# DARKSTAR — Tech Architecture

**Doc ID:** TECH-001 · **Version:** 1.4 · **Date:** 2026-11-18 (§2.7–§2.9, §3.4, §8.1, §9, §11.2: audio change set C1–C7 built; **cosmic horror design rev 3:** §3.4, §5.1–§5.3, §11.1 Q-T66–Q-T73, §11.1a, §11.2; **Act 1 vertical slice (PM ruling 2026-11-18, owner grant):** §11.1 Q-T100–Q-T103 (Q-T102, Q-T103 resolved the same day after The Reviewer's notes), §11.2 six decision rows, the PR D hand-off row's `skip_curtain` citation Q-T55 → Q-T60 and the CH-7 row's citation Q-T57 → Q-T62) · **Owner:** production · **Status:** complete-draft
**Upstream:** `GDD_DARKSTAR_v0.2.md` (GDD-001), `SYSTEMS_BIBLE_STATS.md` (SYS-001), `ZONES_AND_ENCOUNTERS.md` (ZONES-001), `ART_BIBLE.md` (ART-001), `SPRITE_MODULARITY_SPEC.md` (ART-002) · **Downstream:** every code module, every `.tres`, the CI lint, the build pipeline
**Number discipline (hard rule of this doc).** SYS-001 is the only source of numbers. A number appearing here is one of exactly two things: (a) a SYS-001 value being *cited* by section, or (b) an **engineering parameter** we invented here — budget splits, cache sizes, pool sizes, tick rates — marked `✱` and owned by production. An `✱` parameter may never compete with a SYS-001 number; where the two touch, SYS-001 wins and this doc re-cuts its split.

---

## 0. How to read this doc set

### 0.1 What each doc owns

| Doc | Path | Owns | Does **not** own |

| **This doc (TECH-001)** | `docs/04-production/TECH_ARCHITECTURE.md` | Engine and build config; the Godot scene tree and node ownership; the anchor-node contracts; the data-resource layout and the `ds_` key convention; the tunable-class rules and the data lint; the seeded-RNG and replay design; the shader stack and the draw-call/memory ceilings; manifest **consumption** at build time; save layout, versioning, migration, telemetry keys, Steamworks scope; the per-frame budget split; the automated-test set | Any game number (SYS-001). Any art rule (ART-001/ART-002). Any level parameter (ZONES-001). Any run-structure rule (LOOP-001). Any lore claim (NARR-001) |
| Game design document | `docs/01-design/GDD_DARKSTAR_v0.2.md` | What the game is; the pillars; the systems as fiction and as player experience; the locked decisions (GDD §18) and the glossary (GDD §19) | Numbers, scene structure, file formats |

| Zones & encounters | `docs/01-design/ZONES_AND_ENCOUNTERS.md` | Biomes, the 32 Encounter Kits, kit `.tres` parameters, the seam assembler's rules, the **anchor-node names** (§1.7) and the geometry contracts each anchor carries | The node classes that implement those anchors; budgets |

ASSET-003 is being written by the art owner and does not exist on disk yet. Everything in §6 that would otherwise live there is stated here as a **consumer-side contract**: what TECH-001 reads, in what shape, and what it does when the read fails. If ASSET-003 contradicts §6 on a producer-side detail, ASSET-003 wins; on a consumption detail, this doc wins.

### 0.2 Conventions used in this doc

| Convention | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `🔒` | Locked by GDD-001 §18 or SYS-001; code may assert it, never vary it |
| `⚙` | Tunable in playtest; lives in a data resource; hot-reloadable |
| `✱` | Invented here, first-pass engineering parameter, owned by production, expected to move |
| `SYS-001 §n` | A citation, not a copy. If the row moves, this doc does not need to change |
| status column | Every claim about what exists is per-row. Prose never claims a system is "done" |

### 0.3 New technical vocabulary introduced by this doc

Game-world words come only from GDD §19. The following are **engineering** names and mean nothing in-fiction.

| Term | Definition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `SectorHost` | The scene and script that own one sector's lifetime: load, populate, run, tear down | §2.1 |
| `SeamAssembler` | The run-time component that picks kits and stitches them into a Seam per ZONES-001 §4 | §2.2 |
| `KitAssembly` | The instantiated result of one Encounter Kit `.tres`: geometry plus filled slots plus anchors | §2.2 |
| `SlotFill` | One generator decision — a unit placed on a spawn marker — recorded deterministically | §2.2 |
| `AnchorNode` | A named marker/area node in a kit carrying a fixed contract (ZONES-001 §1.6) | §2.3 |
| `CompositeRig` | The runtime node graph that assembles one unit or the entity from 64×64 modules | §2.4 |
| `TunableRegistry` | The single load-time table that resolves every `ds_` key to a value plus its class and SYS-001 row | §3.2 |
| `ds_` key | The dotted, namespaced addressable name of one tunable (§3.4) | §3 |
| `ManifestImporter` | **Retired (D-13).** Was: the build step turning `SPRITE_MANIFEST.csv` into a GDScript table | §6.3 |
| `AtlasTable` | **Retired (D-13).** Was: the lookup from part id to atlas region | §6.3 |
| `WarpComposer` | The per-frame owner of which canvas-item shaders are active at the current Warp Tier | §5.1 |
| `DiegeticHud` | The HUD layer built from in-world objects so it can lie, be eaten, and be drawn by the same code as honest data | §5.2 |
| `SteadyLedger` | The accessibility option that forces the HUD honest | §5.3 |
| `RunSeed`, `SeedCode`, `ReplayVerifier` | Deterministic run identity, the 12-character shareable form, and the hash-comparison test. `RunSeed` and `SeedCode` built (`core/rng/`); `ReplayVerifier` not built (needs a deterministic sim) | §4.9 |
| `SaveEnvelope` | The versioned wrapper around every persisted block | §7.1 |
| `ContactBudget` | The runtime counter enforcing SYS-001 §9's on-screen caps (contacts, units, shots) | §8.2 |
| `FrameBudget` | The per-system split of the 16.7 ms frame | §8.1 |

## 1. Engine and build

### 1.1 Locked by the GDD

| Item | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Engine | Godot **4.7.2 stable**, 2D renderer | GDD §2, §12; D-06 |
| Language | **GDScript only** for gameplay | D-06 |
| Internal resolution | **Logical space 640 × 360** for the whole app (root window, `canvas_items`). The **Act 0 arcade screen** is a pixel-perfect low-res grid in its own `SubViewport` (`ArcadeScreen`), size from `CabinetScreenSpec` (`ds_cabinet.screen.grid`, ✱): **256×304 portrait, 4 bpp (the Williams video circuit with the display rotated 90°; owner 2026-11-18, replaces the 240×320 approval of the same day), built in PR B**, with a **40 px HUD strip** on top and a 256×264 play view (`CabinetScreenSpec`). **Owner video limits, layer one only (the Act 0 screen: never the bezel, never after Act 0), approved 2026-11-18, BUILT 2026-11-18 as `VideoRegisters` + `ScreenRegisters` (§2.10, §11.2):** (1) 16 colours on screen at once, chosen from a 256-colour palette at 3 bits red, 3 bits green, 2 bits blue (RGB332); (2) the colour registers are writable by the CPU at any time, so colour cycling and fades are register writes; (3) frame time 16.64 ms, from a 14-bit video counter driven by a 1 MHz clock and reloaded with 0x3F00 the first time it overflows (16640 counts); the 16.64 ms clock drives register cycling only, gameplay physics stays at 60 Hz (`physics_ticks_per_second` is an integer; 60.096 Hz is impossible, 0.16 % apart). The limits fade out as `Look.t` goes 0 to 1 (THE PRETENCE; in-fiction, ART-001 §15). Register table and Pal-to-register map: ART-001 §15.2a. The bezel and everything after Act 0 render at the display's native resolution (D-A, §11.2) | SYS-001 §0; ART-001 §1; GDD §2 |
| Filtering | nearest-neighbour, never mipmapped, **inside the Act 0 screen** (`ScreenViewport` and its container); root default is Linear so the vector bezel is not nearest-upscaled; after Act 0 the filter is per asset (open, §11.1 Q3) | ART-002 §1 |
| Scaling | Root: `canvas_items`, aspect `expand`, `fractional`, 2D snapping off. Act 0 screen: integer step only, `k = max(1, floor(min(W/640, H/360)))` display px per screen px, computed by `ScreenFit` (`core/ui/screen_fit.gd`, pure static; k, rig transform, hole rect, `bezel_rect`, window↔screen mapping), re-run on window resize. **Implemented (PR A, 2026-11-18; `bezel_rect` PR B)** | §1.3 |
| Logical space | 640×360 stays the logical coordinate space, so SYS-001 px and px/s keep their meaning (1 logical px = display height / 360; on 16:9 one Act 0 screen px = one logical px). Post-Act 0 camera: zoom 1 = the 640×360 logical frame, fixed (Z1, resolved 2026-11-18, §11.1 Q-T25, §11.2); ultrawide extra width is cosmetic, telegraphs and spawns stay inside 640×360; scale comes from `Parallax2D` far layers | D-A |
| Target frame rate | 60 FPS; 60 Hz fixed logic tick | SYS-001 §0, §9 |
| Platform at 1.0 | **Windows + Steam** | GDD §2, §12 |
| Platforms after 1.0 | Linux, then Mac | GDD §2 |
| Data-driven | every table in SYS-001 maps to a `.tres`/JSON resource | D-06, SYS-001 header |
| Mono / C# | not used | D-06 |
| Multiplayer | none in the build; no `MultiplayerAPI`, no network scene, no `networking/` folder (the godot-ai editor addon's loopback link is editor tooling, never in the game build) | D-09 (coop out of scope for 1.0), GDD R-07 |
| Rendering method | **Forward+** (`rendering/renderer/rendering_method = forward_plus`) | owner decision 2026-11-17 (TASK-BOOT Q2); revisit only if min-spec shows T2 warp shaders over SYS-001 §9's 1.2 ms |

**Amended (owner, 2026-11-18, D-A):** the low-res grid binds the Act 0 arcade screen only; the low-res look is a skin/pretence, not a technical limit. The cabinet bezel is native-resolution print art (D-B) and leaves the frame at the end of Act 0 by a camera zoom-in (the world spills out of the broken tube, §11.2). The stretch/snap/filter settings in §1.2 and the screen SubViewport (§2.10) are **implemented** (PR A). Phases: PR A (ScreenFit, SubViewport screen, `canvas_items`) built; PR B (256×304 grid, readouts on the screen strip, 640/360 literals removed from screen-side code; built on branch claude/act0-portrait-grid), PR C (`VectorText`, Sinistar-style native bezel, control-panel buttons) and PR D (zoom destruction) **not built**. Post-Act 0 camera/world behaviour is not built.

The GDD §2 row offering "512×288 **or** 640×360" is closed here: **640×360** (for the Act 0 screen and as the logical space; see above). It is the resolution SYS-001's px values are written against and the one ART-002's module grid composes against (360 px = 5.625 modules of height; the entity composite at 512×256 therefore cannot fit the frame, which is ART-001 §2's scale-dread rule working by arithmetic rather than by staging).

### 1.2 Project settings we set and never revisit

| Setting | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `display/window/size/viewport_width` | 640 | §1.1 |
| `display/window/size/viewport_height` | 360 | §1.1 |
| `display/window/stretch/mode` | `canvas_items` | the bezel is native-res vector; only the Act 0 screen is integer-scaled (`ScreenFit`, §1.3) |
| `display/window/stretch/aspect` | `expand` | no bars; the bezel bleeds into extra width/height |
| `display/window/stretch/scale_mode` | `fractional` | the screen's integer step is applied by `ScreenFit`, not the engine |
| `display/window/hidpi/nearest_filtering` | off | native-res bezel must not be nearest-upscaled by the OS |
| `rendering/2d/snap/snap_2d_transforms_to_pixel`, `…snap_2d_vertices_to_pixel` | off (root) | on per viewport: `ScreenViewport` sets its own snap flags on |
| `rendering/anti_aliasing/quality/msaa_2d` | 4× ✱ (root only; `ScreenViewport` has MSAA off) | `draw_polygon` has no AA; vector bezel edges need it. Cost is a §8.1 line, measured under Q-T24 |
| `rendering/textures/canvas_textures/default_texture_filter` | `Linear` (root); `Nearest` inside the screen (`ScreenViewport` and `ArcadeScreen`) | vector bezel at native res; ART-002 §1 holds inside the screen |
| physics ticks per second | 60 | SYS-001 §0 counts frames at 60 Hz |
| `physics/common/physics_ticks_frozen` | never | the game freezes *entities*, never physics (§4.4) |
| `application/boot_methods` | none | boot is a scene, so the arcade shell is the first thing drawn |
| max FPS | uncapped in menus, 60 in-sector | sector logic is tick-locked |
| vsync | on | tearing reads as a warp artefact and would be indistinguishable from content |
| `audio/driver/output_latency` | 60 ms requested | SYS-001 §9 input-to-effect ≤ 60 ms |
| `input_devices/pointing/emulate_touch_from_mouse` | off | a stray touch drag would fire the lance |
| `vr/xr` | not enabled | — |

`Physics` uses Godot's 2D default with one custom exception: geodes and the entity's segments are `StaticBody2D` with one-way `Shape2D` per module, not per composite (§2.4). This keeps a knocked-off segment's collision leaving in the same frame the module leaves the rig.

### 1.3 Integer scaling, honestly — the Act 0 screen

The integer law now binds the **Act 0 arcade screen** only. `ScreenFit` picks `k = max(1, floor(min(W/640, H/360)))` and draws the screen grid at exactly `k` display px per texel, centred in the window. **No black bars at any aspect (owner, 2026-11-18):** `ScreenFit.bezel_rect` is the whole window in rig units (hole centred), and the bezel fills it (`BezelField` plus the columns, §2.10). Everything else is native resolution (§1.1).

Grid **256×304** (owner, 2026-11-18; the Williams circuit rotated 90°), hole `(192,28,256,304)` in 640×360 units. Test sizes come from the Steam Hardware Survey (Oct 2026) plus the Steam Deck:

| Window | k | Screen shown at | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1366×768, 1280×800 (Steam Deck) | 2 | 512×608 | |
| 1920×1080, 1920×1200 | 3 | 768×912 | |
| 2560×1440, 2560×1600, 3440×1440 | 4 | 1024×1216 | ultrawide and 16:10 get more bezel, never bars |
| 3840×2160 | 6 | 1536×1824 | |

The tests (`core/ui/tests/test_screen_fit.gd`, `features/cabinet/tests/test_bezel_art.gd`) cover these eight sizes. Weak case: a window just under a multiple (for example 1600×900, k=2) shows the screen smaller against the window; the integer law wins.

We refuse non-integer scaling **of the screen**. A fractional scale blurs a 1 px outline, and the 1 px `VOID-0` outline is the last line of legibility when the warp layers start distorting shape (ART-001 §2). The frame around the screen is the native-resolution bezel (ART-001 §12.7a), not bars. A player who wants a smaller or larger screen gets a different integer step, not a fractional scale; the option is labelled by the multiple it produces. The sim never sees `k`.

**Global shader uniform `cab_screen_light` (PR C pass 2, 2026-11-19).** The screen lights the cabinet: `ScreenLight` (§2.10) writes one global colour uniform (rgb = light colour, a = intensity) that the bezel shaders read (declared in `cabinet_material.gdshaderinc`). It is registered in `project.godot` `[shader_globals]` because a shader that uses a global uniform fails to compile if the global does not exist; any new shader using it needs no extra setup, and a new global needs the same registration. Read-only with respect to the screen.

**Drawing rule: 1 px lines are drawn pixel-centred (2026-11-18, found in S6).** A 1 px `draw_line` / `draw_polyline` between integer grid coordinates is a 1 px-wide quad through pixel *corners*; on a near-45° run it covers no pixel centre and rasterises **nothing** (the S6 coroner's outline and arrow barbs vanished in a real windowed run while every unit test passed). On the integer grid, 1 px strokes use coordinates offset by +0.5 (pixel centres): `FxLayer.chalk_line_ends(a, b)` / `_chalk_line()` is the reference helper. **`draw_arc` has the same problem (2026-11-19, found in the S6 follow-up):** a 1 px `draw_arc` at an integer centre rasterises the same way (the shooter ring never rendered), so any 1 px arc or ring is drawn as **pixel-centred chords** through `_chalk_line`, not `draw_arc` (reference: `FxLayer.death_ring_segments`). Regression test: `features/cabinet/tests/test_readable_death.gd` `test_real_entity_death_in_the_real_stage_draws_the_outline_and_arrow` (asserts every chalk endpoint is pixel-centred, since headless has no pixels to read). This binds every 1 px vector on the Act 0 screen and bezel (bezel lines, `VectorText`/`PixelText` strokes, the video-limits work). Existing 1 px strokes elsewhere are not yet audited (a follow-up for their owners); ART-001 §14's Vector row ("coordinates snapped to whole pixels") should gain the same clarification (Visual Design to word it).

| Target | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Windows x86-64, Steam Depot 1 | planned | only shipping target for 1.0 |
| Windows demo build | planned | Steam playable/demo branch, same binary, gated content flag |
| Linux x86-64 | post-1.0 | not built or exported during development; the CI job (§9.2) already runs the full headless suite on Linux, which covers the compile check |
| macOS | post-1.0 | out of scope for v1 entirely |
| Steam Deck verification | target, not gate | verified after the vertical slice; the ×2/40-bar case above is the one we test first |
| Web | tested 2026-11-18 (boots; single run) | low priority, not a release target; inherits the desktop tests |
| Console / mobile | refused | refused, not deferred |

| Build step | Runs | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `import` | every checkout, first run | Godot `.import` cache; regenerated, never committed |
| `manifest_import` | **retired** (D-13) | no raster sprites are consumed; the manifest is a reference inventory only (ASSET-003) |
| `data_lint` | pre-build, CI (planned; not built, the CI job §9.2 runs the suite only) | every SYS-001 §10 row ↔ exactly one `ds_` key; class check (§3.3) |
| `palette_lint` | test suite | `Pal` = the palette file (built); generator render tests check kit colour pools (ART-001 §14.7, planned) |
| `localize_extract` | deferred | English only (L10N-001 §1); `locale/en.po` is edited directly |
| `export_presets` | release, QA | `export_presets.cfg` is committed and credential-free (signing/Steam values go in a local override, never committed). Two presets, Windows Desktop, PCK embedded, no encryption: `Windows Desktop` (release, `build/windows/DARKSTAR.exe`) and `Windows QA` (feature `automation_qa`, `build/windows_qa/DARKSTAR_QA.exe`). Filters and the guard test: §9.1 "Profiles and presets". Export: `--headless --path . --export-release "<preset>" <exe>`; templates for 4.7.2.stable must be installed |

Modders: we ship the `.tres` files loose in the PCK's `data/` folder so a player can edit a tunable in a text editor, and we ship no mod API, no loader hooks and no Steam Workshop at 1.0. Editing a `.tres` is supported as a supported-as-is affordance and is deliberately not a feature; §3.3 rejects a save that carries an out-of-class edit.

### 1.5 GDScript versus native — the honest boundary

| Work | Where it runs | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Movement, lance, charges, Composure, Warp Tier state, Hourglass Clock, spawn budgets, Choir AI state machines, Lantern Tree, save, telemetry, HUD, menus, dialogue, Codex, sector assembly | **GDScript** | all of it is state machines over small numbers, bounded by SYS-001 §3 spawn budgets and §9 contact ceilings — not by arithmetic volume |
| Per-pixel work (light masks, Shroud, warp distortion) | **canvas-item shaders**, GLSL-like Godot shader language | GPU-side; no GDScript pixel loops ever |
| Composite atlas lookups, part pooling | **GDScript**, table-driven | lookups are dictionary hits, not per-pixel work |
| Anything measured over 10,000 objects per frame | would need GDExtension/Rust or C++ | **the ceiling is never reached**: SYS-001 §9 caps on-screen Choir units, entity modules, geodes, hostile contacts, active shots and colliders per sector (all ✱ first-pass rows added 2026-11-17, ruling R-14) |
| A native collision or spatial-hash rewrite | refused for v1 | Godot's 2D physics is expected to be inside budget at SYS-001 §9's collider ceiling; §8.3 measures before we decide |
| Steamworks | GDExtension (`GDSteamworks`, community build, pinned version ✱) | the one exception we accept, because it is a binding, not gameplay code |

**We refuse to write native code for v1.** Not because GDScript is fast, but because nothing in this game's per-frame work is arithmetic-bound: the frame cost is draw calls, shader invocations and scene churn, and those are engine-side regardless of what language authors them. A GDScript hot spot found in §8 profiling gets a data-structure fix or a move to a shader first; a GDExtension rewrite is a documented, logged decision with a profile attached, and no such decision has been taken as of this doc. The single native component we will ship is the pinned Steamworks binding, which contains no game logic and can be stubbed out for non-Steam builds.

Risk we state rather than hide: GDScript is interpreted, and the two places where a naive implementation *would* break budget are the shard-magnet loop (§4.3) and the Ledger Compass redraw (§5.4). Both are designed to be table- and cache-driven in §8.4 rather than per-frame scans. If either misses budget, that is the first candidate for a §11 open question about a GDExtension exception, and it will be a decision with a profile attached, not a refactor done quietly.

---

## 2. Scene tree and node ownership

Status legend for every table below: **built** = exists and is covered by a test; **stub** = exists, minimal; **planned** = designed here, no code.

### 2.1 Top-level tree

```
root (Window, 640×360 logical)
├─ SettingsService, SaveService, SceneRouter      autoloads (§2.8)
└─ Boot  (scenes/boot/boot.tscn — main scene, lives for the whole session)
   ├─ RouteHost          exactly one route scene at a time (mounted by SceneRouter)
   │   └─ AttractMode | CabinetGame (Act 0, §2.10) | RuinsStub | SectorStub (SectorHost, Act 1 stub) | AetherArrival (§2.9, stub) | later: LanternTree…
   └─ CurtainLayer (CanvasLayer 100) / Curtain     fade between routes
```

| Node / scene | Owns | Never owns | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Boot` | route host, transition curtain, `-- --smoke` boot path | game state, menus | built |
| Route scene (e.g. `AttractMode`) | its own Controls, its `ScreenStack` of overlays, an `InputModeTracker` | other routes, saves | built |
| `SectorStub` = `SectorHost` stub (`scenes/sector/`) | sector lifetime: spawn the Vigil at `VigilSpawn`, the sector `torus` (Q-T58: size from `CabinetStage.field_size` via `arena_source`; injected into `SectorCamera`, `SectorWrap` and the compass `world_rect`; no boundary walls), the `SectorFloor` (`data/sector/sector_floor.tres`, ✱ visual values), HUD readout, pause, the `SectorCamera` and the `LedgerCompass` stub (M1 slice 3 PR 1); `GeodeSandbox`, `ShardField` and `ChargeMagazine` (M1 slice 3 PR 3; the torus is injected into the first two, the Vigil is the magnet collector and the sandbox reference); sets `vigil.emitter.projectile_parent = %Actors` so Arc-Lance shots live in the sector, not under Boot (or null in tests) | run state (future `run` feature), persistence | stub |
| `Vigil` (`features/vigil/vigil.tscn`) | its components (§2.5, §2.6) | camera, HUD, input map | built |
| Overlays (`PauseScreen`, `SettingsScreen`, `ConfirmScreen`) | their buttons; emit intent signals | the tree's pause state (owned by the scene that pushed them) | built |

Dev routes (2026-11-18): `data/routes/dev_route_table.tres` (today only `aether_gallery`, scene `scenes/dev/aether_gallery/`) is merged by Boot (`_merge_dev_routes()`) into the router with `SceneRouter.merge_routes()` only when `Boot._is_dev_build()` is true (feature tag `editor`, `automation_dev` or `automation_qa`, the §9.1 gate). `merge_routes` refuses to overwrite an existing id; the shipped route table never names a dev scene (test `tests/test_dev_routes_gate.gd`). The release export excludes `scenes/dev/*` and `data/routes/dev_*` (set on the automation chat's branch; check the preset after merge). **Route param `skip_curtain` (built 2026-11-18, closes Q-T60):** `SceneRouter.go_to(id, {"skip_curtain": true, …})` skips the host's `transition_out` and `transition_in`; the remove, mount and `on_route_entered(params)` already run in one deferred call, so the old scene's last frame is followed by the new scene's first (no curtain, no hard cut to black). Only a bool `true` under a String or StringName key counts (`SceneRouter.skips_curtain(params)`); every other route change, and reduced motion, keeps the curtain as before. Boot needs no change (`mount` removes and adds in one call). Used by the Pretence → Aether swap to the shipped route `&"aether"` (`features/aether/aether_arrival.tscn`, §2.9) with `{aether_time_s, tier, camera_centre, camera_zoom, skip_curtain: true}`. Tests: `tests/test_route_skip_curtain.gd`, `features/aether/tests/test_aether_arrival.gd`. **Route param `continue_stage` (2026-11-19, Q-T104):** int, 0-based; absent or −1 means none. Accepted by `&"attract"` (shows the CONTINUE page) and `&"cabinet"` (continue at that stage); attract side built, cabinet side owned by the Arcade layer session (§2.10).

Rules: a route is addressed by id from `data/routes/route_table.tres` (plus the dev table above in dev builds), never by path in code. Overlays are not routes. `get_tree().paused` is set only by the scene that owns the overlay stack (`SectorStub`), and is cleared on its `_exit_tree`. Node lookups: `%UniqueName` inside a scene, `@export` references across a scene boundary, groups for "find all of a kind" (`vigil`), never `get_node("../..")`.

### 2.2 SeamAssembler / KitAssembly — planned
Picks Encounter Kits and stitches them into a Seam per ZONES-001 §4. Runs inside `SectorHost`, never as an autoload. Records every `SlotFill` against the `RunSeed` (§4.9) so a run replays. Not built.

### 2.3 AnchorNode — planned
Anchor names and contracts are ZONES-001 **§1.7** (the kit scene tree: `GravityAnchor`, `HeraldAnchor`, `RiteGateAnchor`, `PenitentAnchor`, `UiEatAnchor`, `SpawnMarkerLit/Unlit`, `LitanyRingNodes`, `EscapeVector`, `WrapEdge`). A kit contains markers only, never unit instances (ZONES-001 §1.7). Code finds anchors by group named after the anchor type, never by path. Not built.

### 2.4 CompositeRig — planned
Assembles a unit from 64×64 **generated** parts (ART-002 §5, ART-001 §14.3: vector or shader generators with sockets, D-13). One `StaticBody2D`/`Area2D` shape per module (§1.2), so a knocked-off segment loses collision on the same frame. Not built.

### 2.5 Movement and animation — responsibility split (audit)

Audit of the approved mechanics (GDD §4, SYS-001 §1.1, §9, WARP-001 §2, ART-002 §5):

| Motion need | Applies? | Owner | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vigil thrust / drag / brake / snap turn | yes (SYS-001 §1.1) | `VigilMotor` in `_physics_process` on a `CharacterBody2D` (floating mode); the body never rotates, the `Facing` node does | built |
| Surgelight dash, Anchor freeze field | yes | future `VigilMotor` states / `features/vigil` components | planned |
| Starts, stops, full reversal (0.28 s 🔒 derived) | yes | `VigilMotor.step_velocity` (pure, frame-rate independent); tested | built |
| Choir locomotion, processions | yes | per-unit motor components; paths from kit anchors | planned |
| Modular rig mutation, segment loss | yes (ART-002 §5.3) | `CompositeRig` swaps atlas regions; physics shapes per module (§2.4) | planned |
| Sprite frame animation | yes | `AnimationPlayer` (frame swaps, method tracks that *request* events) | planned |
| Lance / charge / shard VFX | yes | `GPUParticles2D` (small, physical, ART-001 §7) + pooled `Line2D` | planned |
| Per-pixel warp distortion | yes | canvas-item shaders driven by `WarpComposer` (§5.1) | planned |
| UI transitions | yes | `Tween`, 150–300 ms, instant under Reduced Motion (UI-001 §4) | built |
| Camera follow, shake, zoom | yes (SYS-001 §9) | `SectorCamera` (`features/sector/sector_camera.gd`, a `Camera2D` stepped in the physics tick) with `SectorCameraStats` (`data/sector/sector_camera.tres`, `ds_camera.*`, §3.4): follow 0.12 s, 24 px deadzone, 1.5 % aim lookahead, shake ≤ 4 px / 0.25 s, zoom 1.0 (Q-T25 resolved Z1), shake off under Reduced Motion ✱; warp shake inside the ceiling. Follow readings accepted (Q-T55..Q-T57, §11.1a): lookahead = fraction of the viewport per axis, 0.12 s = smoothing time (omega 2/T), 24 px = circular deadzone radius. The M1 sandbox is a wrapping torus 1920×720 ✱ (Q-T58; same source as the Cabinet field, `CabinetStage.field_size`): `SectorStub.torus` is injected into the camera, which follows the target by `Torus.delta` (deadzone and lookahead goal) and rebases focus and position by whole-field shifts when the Vigil wraps, so the view never jumps; `SectorWrap` wraps the Vigil after its physics step (velocity untouched) and keeps other actors on the image nearest the Vigil; `SectorFloor` draws a repeating `Pal` floor over the whole view (no clear colour) | **built** (follow + deadzone + lookahead + shake API + torus seam; no shake is triggered yet; T3/T5 zoom not built) |
| Jumps, landings, slopes, stairs, ladders | **N/A** — top-down space game | — | — |
| Skeletons, IK, blend spaces, `AnimationTree` | **N/A** — modular sprite rigs, no bones | — | — |
| Facial animation / lip-sync | **N/A** — entity lines are big type + audio (GDD §10) | — | — |
| Vehicles, ragdoll, cloth | **N/A** | — | — |
| Multiplayer authority | **N/A** (D-09) | — | — |

**Gameplay drives animation, never the reverse.** State lives in components (motor, health, future AI state machines). An `AnimationPlayer` reads that state to pick a clip. A method track may *request* a gameplay event (open a hitbox window, play a thruster cue, spawn a spark), but the component validates and owns the outcome. A skipped or sped-up animation can never change a hit, a timing, or a save.

Footstep-equivalents (thruster, mining ticks), audio cues (AUDIO-001 §6.2), VFX and camera feedback subscribe to gameplay signals (EVT-001), not to animation frames, except where a method track is the request source.

Reusable animation resources: animation libraries per kit under `features/<feature>/anim/` (planned). Import settings: §10. Movement/transition tests: §9.

### 2.6 Collision and hit detection (audit)

| Layer | Name | Who is on it | Masks it | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `world` | sector walls, geodes, entity segments (per module), the Sepulchre | Vigil, Choir bodies, Null-Iron Charges | built (walls; geode hurtboxes, M1 slice 3 PR 3; Sepulchre hurtbox, `bites = false`, PR 4) |
| 2 | `vigil` | Vigil `CharacterBody2D` | — | built |
| 3 | `choir` | Choir unit bodies | — | planned |
| 4 | `vigil_shots` | Arc-Lance hitboxes / shots, Null-Iron Charges | `choir_hurtbox`, `world`, `shroud` | built: `LanceProjectile` and `NullIronCharge` (M1 slice 3 PR 4) both sit here and mask `world` + `choir_hurtbox` (`Area2D` overlap; `shroud` not built) |
| 5 | `choir_shots` | Choir projectiles | `vigil_hurtbox` | planned |
| 6 | `pickups` | Mourning Glass | Vigil pickup area | planned |
| 7 | `interactables` | shrines, Sepulchre deposit, Chalk Desk | Vigil interact area | planned |
| 8 | `shroud` | Shroud terrain (SYS-001 §9) | Vigil body, lance | planned |
| 9 | `vigil_hurtbox` | Vigil `HurtboxComponent` | `choir_shots`, hazards | built |
| 10 | `choir_hurtbox` | Choir hurtboxes | `vigil_shots` | built (Oubliette Grub biting hurtbox, M1 slice 3 PR 4) |

Source: `core/physics/physics_layers.gd` (`PhysicsLayers`) and `project.godot [layer_names]` — keep them identical.

| Need | Shape / node | Rule | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Vigil movement | `CircleShape2D` on `CharacterBody2D`; radius = `ds_vigil.move.hitbox_circle_px` / 2 | collision from data, never from the sprite; `move_and_slide` sweeps, so no tunnelling | built; tested at 6000 px/s |
| Vigil hurtbox | `CircleShape2D` on `HurtboxComponent` (same radius) | hurtboxes are `monitorable`, never `monitoring` | built |
| Lance bite filter | `HurtboxComponent.bites: bool = true` (`@export`); `LanceProjectile.can_bite(target)` (static) | the lance does no damage to a hurtbox with `bites = false`, but the shot is still used up; other hitters (e.g. a Charge's `receive_hit`) ignore the flag (PM ruling A2) | built (M1 slice 3 PR 2) |
| Enemy hitbox | sprite alpha ∩ 1 px inset (SYS-001 §9) → baked primitive/convex per module at import | convex only where a primitive cannot fit; no triangle mesh on anything that moves | planned |
| Walls, segments | `StaticBody2D`, rectangle/circle/convex | concave/segment shapes only for static kit geometry | built (walls) |
| Geodes (M1) | `SectorGeode`: a `HurtboxComponent` (`Area2D`, `CircleShape2D` of the SYS-001 §1.3 radius per size class) on layer `world`, mask 0, `bites = true`, forwarding to a `HealthComponent` (one hit per attack window). **No `StaticBody2D`**: the Vigil flies through geodes. Reason: a solid body would stop the Arc-Lance shot (it is spent on contact) before the hurtbox took the damage. Design confirmation open (§11.1 Q-T76) | mining and over-fire are counted from accepted hurtbox hits only | built (M1 slice 3 PR 3) |
| Oubliette Grub (M1) | `OublietteGrub`: a biting `HurtboxComponent` (`CircleShape2D`, radius SYS-001 §3 Grub body row) on `choir_hurtbox`, mask 0, forwarding to a `HealthComponent` (SYS-001 §3 HP). No body: Grubs steer as `Node2D`s, torus-aware. A Charge touching it is intercepted (`charge_lost`) | built (M1 slice 3 PR 4) |
| Sepulchre (M1, phases 0–1) | `Sepulchre`: a `HurtboxComponent` (`CircleShape2D`, SYS-001 §2 Sepulchre hurt radius) on `world`, mask 0, **`bites = false`, no `HealthComponent`**: the lance leaves a chalk tick and is spent; a Null-Iron Charge hit (kind `null_iron_charge`) knocks SYS-001 §2 row 0's 1 segment off (`segment_knocked`, SYS-001 §7.1 score) | built (M1 slice 3 PR 4) |
| Null-Iron Charge (M1) | `NullIronCharge`: an `Area2D` (`CircleShape2D`, SYS-001 §1.4 Charge contact radius) on `vigil_shots`, mask `world` + `choir_hurtbox`. Homes on its target hurtbox with a turn limit of π / SYS-001 §1.4 turn authority rad/s. Ends HIT (target reached, `charge_hit`), LOST (Grub or geode contact takes its SYS-001 §1.4 HP to 0, `charge_lost`) or GUTTERED (SYS-001 §1.4 Charge life, `charge_gutter`). At the SYS-001 §1.4 Charge speed it moves less than its own diameter per 60 Hz tick, so the sweep rule below does not apply | built (M1 slice 3 PR 4) |
| Fast shots, the lance | `ShapeCast2D` / `RayCast2D` swept each physics tick from previous to current position | anything moving more than its own size per tick must sweep | planned |
| Attack windows | `HitboxComponent.open_window()/close_window()` | one hit per target per window, enforced twice (hitbox set + `HealthComponent` window key) | built |
| Moving platforms, doors, stairs, slopes, ragdolls, cloth, vehicles | **N/A** to this game | — | — |

Ownership: all hit truth resolves in `_physics_process` (60 Hz, §1.2). Animation never toggles shapes directly. Expensive bodies (future Choir) disable processing when off-screen beyond the audio-occlusion radius (SYS-001 §9) — planned, to be measured in §8. Single-player only (D-09): there is no authority or rollback layer.

Bullet-layer decision (TASK-BOOT Q1, owner 2026-11-17): Godot `Area2D`/`ShapeCast2D` first. GDD §13's custom quadtree is built only if §8 profiling shows the bullet layer missing budget at SYS-001 §9's active-shot ceiling.

### 2.7 Audio buses
`data/audio/default_bus_layout.tres`: `Master` (= AUDIO-001's `BUS-MST`; Godot requires bus 0 to be the master), `BUS-MUS`, `BUS-CHO`, `BUS-ENT`, `BUS-UI`, `BUS-FLY`, `BUS-AMB`, all sending to `Master`. Ducking sidechains (AUDIO-001 §7.2) and the never-ducked rules (§7.3) are **not wired yet**. `AudioService` is built (§2.8). Its 24 one-shot voices are routed per cue by the policy below (menu, H and I cues to `BUS-UI`; `wake_hit` and `entity_unmade` to `BUS-ENT`; `grub_keen` to `BUS-CHO`; the rest to `BUS-FLY`); the chant stays on `BUS-CHO`. All buses are at 0 dB today, and no code uses `BUS-MUS`. `BUS-AMB` is used by the ambience slot and the two Aether one-shots (AUDIO-001 §12.12).

**Built 2026-11-18 (AUDIO-001 §12.7 C1, C2, C6; owner-approved):**
- **Voice policy.** `core/audio/cue_policy.gd` (`CuePolicy`, a `TunableResource`, `ds_namespace` `ds_audio`, an `entries` table, no `ds_` keys yet), `core/audio/cue_policy_entry.gd`, and `data/audio/cue_policy.tres` (61 entries; 46 before AUDIO-001 §12.15; every value is ✱, engineering-only). Per entry: `bus`, `priority`, `max_concurrent`, `min_retrigger_ms`, `pitch_jitter_cents`. The total stays 24 voices (AUDIO-001 §7.5). Sizzle additions (2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §12.11), all ✱ and in this same table or as `AudioService` constants, no individual `ds_` keys yet: `lance_dull` and `tally_blip` entries; `lance` `pitch_jitter_cents` 20; chant-bed pitch smoothing `BED_PITCH_FALL_S` 0.15 s (down) and `BED_PITCH_RISE_S` 0.35 s (up; the AUDIO-001 §1.4 ramp floor); chant-bed pitch clamp 0.25 to 1.0.
- **M1 slice 3 cues (2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §12.13; unauditioned), all ✱:** seven new `cue_policy.tres` entries (`overfire_warn`, `overfire_burst`, `magnet_latch`, `charge_armed`, `magazine_full`, `charge_gutter`, `grub_steal`); policy total 34 (verified).
- **Cosmic horror handoffs (2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §12.14; all UNAUDITIONED; L9 to L12, the widened L6 and the Passing PM-approved 2026-11-18, cue numbers still first-pass), all ✱, no individual `ds_` keys yet:** `SUBTRACTION_OPEN_HZ` 20000, `SUBTRACTION_CLOSED_HZ` 500 (exponential low-pass on `BUS-AMB` and `BUS-MUS`), `SUBTRACTION_CUT_DB` −18 (at amount 1.0, same two buses); and twelve new `cue_policy.tres` entries: seven horror cues (`phantom_fire`, `listener_heard`, `matron_birth`, `matron_silenced`, `deceiver_steal`, `presence_flee`, `comforters_murmur`) and five lie-tell ticks (`lie_tell_chalk`, `lie_tell_glyph`, `lie_tell_mirror`, `lie_tell_smear`, `lie_tell_absence`; `BUS-UI`, P1, 1 voice, 250 ms gate); policy total 46 (counted). The telegraph lead (`ds_warp.passing.telegraph_s`) and the T2 echo amount are owned by WARP-001 and passed by the caller, not held here.
- **Heralds and Choir tells (2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §12.15; all UNAUDITIONED), all ✱, no individual `ds_` keys yet:** ten new `cue_policy.tres` entries: `acolyte_tell` (`BUS-CHO`, P1, 3 voices, 0 ms gate, ±40 cents), `phantom_appear` (`BUS-CHO`, P3, 2 voices, 500 ms), `phantom_dispel` (`BUS-CHO`, P3, 2 voices, 100 ms), `herald_arrive` and `herald_reckoning` (`BUS-CHO`, P1, 1 voice, 3000 ms), `herald_dim` and `herald_salt` (`BUS-CHO`, P2, 1 voice, 300 ms), `herald_intercept` (`BUS-FLY`, P2, 1 voice, 200 ms), `charge_intercepted` (`BUS-FLY`, P1, 1 voice), `herald_hush` (`BUS-CHO`, P2, 1 voice, 2000 ms); policy total 56 (46 + 10, counted). No Herald runtime exists; callers are owed by the Choir AI.
- **The Act 0 shift (2026-11-19, AUDIO-001 §12.16; all UNAUDITIONED), all ✱, no individual `ds_` keys yet:** seven new `cue_policy.tres` entries: `censer_bell`, `zone_drift`, `zone_censer_watch`, `zone_shoal`, `zone_stillwater` (was `zone_hollow`), `continue_tick`, `continue_taken` (bus, priority, voices, gate and jitter per cue are in the AUDIO-001 §12.16 table). Policy total **68** (61 + 7, counted); cue ids 74 (67 + 7, counted).
- **Composure wick cues (2026-11-19, AUDIO-001 §12.17; all UNAUDITIONED), all ✱, no individual `ds_` keys yet:** four new `cue_policy.tres` entries, `wick_frayed`, `wick_unmoored`, `wick_hollowed`, `wick_recovery` (E01 to E04; each `BUS-FLY`, P2, 1 voice, 500 ms gate, 0 jitter). Policy total **72** (68 + 4, counted); cue ids 78 (74 + 4, counted). The continuous wick layer is not built.
- **Aether ambience (2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §12.12; unauditioned), all ✱, no individual `ds_` keys yet:** `AMBIENCE_FADE_S` 2.0 s (slot crossfade), `AMBIENCE_MAX_DB` −12 (slot level cap), and two `cue_policy.tres` entries, `aether_swell` and `aether_wrong` (`BUS-AMB`, P5, 1 voice, 2000 ms retrigger gate).
- **Stealing.** A finished voice first, then the lowest-priority stealable voice, then the oldest voice of the same cue. P0 and P1 voices are never stolen; a new cue never steals a more important voice; a P0 or P1 cue at its own cap is dropped. "Finished" is judged from stream length and pitch against an injectable clock (so tests need no real time). Public helpers: `active_count(id)`, `has_cue(id)`. Several picks go beyond AUDIO-001's spec (priorities and caps for `vigil_die`, `grub_keen`, `acolyte_fire`, the Charge, Sepulchre, geode, shard, coin, `extra_life` and `quota` cues); they are ✱ proposals to tune in the M1 slice, listed in AUDIO-001 §12.6.
- **Cue ids.** `core/audio/cue_ids.gd`, `cue_id_entry.gd` and `data/audio/cue_ids.tres` (78 rows; 57 before AUDIO-001 §12.15, 67 before §12.16, 74 before §12.17; counted) list every built cue with its AUDIO-001 §6.2 id or an "Act 0 only" mark. `AudioService` loads the render of each listed id.
- **Fallback.** The `SfxSynth` fallback is built only if a listed cue has no render; with all renders present no synth cue is built. Boot time was not measured (§8.1).

### 2.8 Autoload register

An autoload exists only when the service genuinely needs a global lifetime. Adding one: add a row here first, then `project.godot`.

| Autoload | File | Why global | Must not | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SettingsService` | `autoload/settings_service.gd` | text scale and accessibility apply before the first frame and across scenes (English only; no language setting) | hold game state; read saves | built |
| `SaveService` | `autoload/save_service.gd` | slots outlive scenes; one place enforces SYS-001 §8 cadence | decide *when* to save (callers do); store display text | built |
| `SceneRouter` | `autoload/scene_router.gd` | one top-level scene at a time; any screen may request a change. `merge_routes(table)` (2026-11-18) adds a second route table (the dev table, §2.1) and refuses to overwrite an existing id. Route param `skip_curtain` (2026-11-18, §2.1): a bool `true` skips the host's curtain both ways (`skips_curtain(params)`). Route param `continue_stage` (2026-11-19, §2.10): an int, 0-based stage, on `&"attract"` and `&"cabinet"`; absent or −1 = no CONTINUE offer. Route param `shift_total` (2026-11-19, P1b, §2.10): a non-negative int, the run-scoped Act 0 score total, carried GAME OVER → attract → `&"cabinet"` and cabinet → `&"ruins"` (not consumed yet) | own overlays/modals or UI state | built |
| `RunSession` | `autoload/run_session.gd` | run and Iteration state (LOOP-001 §2) outlive every sector scene across 5 Seams and must be readable by the Ruins and `SaveService`; owns the Hourglass Clock tick (§4.1) | own sector nodes; write saves itself (it hands payloads to `SaveService` at allowed reasons) | **planned** (approved 2026-11-17) |
| `AudioService` | `autoload/audio_service.gd` | music stems, the chant and the entity voice cross scene changes; owns ducking (AUDIO-001 §7.2) | decide *when* a line plays (gameplay signals do) | **built** 2026-11-18: one-shot pool of 24 with priority stealing and per-cue policy (§2.7), chant bed (`set_chant(level)`; `set_bed_pitch_scale(scale)` bends its pitch for the slow-mo, AUDIO-001 §12.11; the autoload never connects to `ArcadeWorld`; `set_ambience(id, level)` drives one Aether ambience slot of two crossfading players on `BUS-AMB`, AUDIO-001 §12.12; `set_subtraction(amount, ramp_s)` / `subtraction()` drive the Passing's low-pass and cut on `BUS-AMB` and `BUS-MUS` only, AUDIO-001 §12.14 (b); the Passing is PM-approved 2026-11-18, no caller = no effect until WARP wires it), silence budget; plays the chirp render of each id in `data/audio/cue_ids.tres` and builds an `SfxSynth` fallback (`core/audio/sfx_synth.gd`) only for a listed cue whose render is missing (§6.5, D-14 amended: no recorded audio, ever) |
| `_mcp_game_helper` | `addons/godot_ai/runtime/game_helper.gd` | editor tooling: lets the godot-ai MCP inspect and drive the game launched from the editor (talks only over Godot's debugger link; returns early in the editor; never consumes input) | hold game state; be referenced by game code; ship in an export (the addon's export plugin strips it; the release preset also excludes `addons/godot_ai/*`) | **built** 2026-11-18 (vendored v4.4.1) |

Refused as autoloads: a UI manager (menus use a per-scene `ScreenStack`), a game manager (state lives in features), a networking service (D-09), a global event bus (EVT-001 §3).
