Every mover (Charges, Grubs, the Sepulchre) lives under `%Actors`, so `SectorWrap` keeps it on the nearest copy |
| inventory | `features/run` (Run Sigils, Relics, Pacts) — no classic inventory/equipment | empty |
| dialogue | `features/entity_voice` (big-type lines; no branching dialogue) | empty |
| quests | **N/A**; nearest: `features/lantern_tree`, `features/codex` | empty |
| interactions | `features/interactions` (shrines, Chalk Desk, Sepulchre deposit) | empty |
| (game-specific) | `features/composure`, `features/warp`, `features/sector`, `features/hud` | sector built (`SectorCamera`, `SectorCameraStats`, §2.5); hud built (`LedgerCompass` stub, `LedgerCompassStats`, §5.4; `ComposureWick` + `ComposureWickStats`, bottom-right of the sector `Hud`, flame height by band only, Q-T119); composure built (M1, Q-T118): `Composure` + `ComposureStats` (0–10, starts 10, clamped, no passive regen, the full SYS-001 §1.5 event API; band by `Composure.band_for`; E01–E04 requested on band change), **sector-scoped** (`%Composure` in `SectorStub`; moves to `RunSession`, §2.8, when that is built); `CockpitFlame` + `CockpitFlameStats` (`%CockpitFlame` under the Vigil's `Facing/Visual`, band by shape only, WARP-001 §4.2 L10). Wired source: a Charge lost to an interceptor (`ChargeLauncher.prune`, −0.5) and a Charge on target (Δ 0); every other row is inert in the sandbox (no source yet). Band effects not built. Readers poll the component; no signals. warp empty |
| the Aether (post-Pretence look, ART-001 §16) | `features/aether` (new 2026-11-18): `AetherLook` (`TunableResource`, `ds_aether.<biome>_t<n>`), `AetherBackdrop` (scene with `set_tier`, `set_drain`, `set_time`, `set_camera`, `apply_look`, and `refresh()` (2026-11-18), which uploads time, camera, screen size, drain and lantern to every layer now without advancing time; `_process` calls it; layers far/mid/near fog bands, crystal sphere, still smudge, leviathan, light shafts, aether-life particles, sky cracks, orrery ribs, entity presence, grade). Shaders in `shaders/aether/`, looks in `data/aether/`, dev gallery in `scenes/dev/aether_gallery/` (dev-only route, §2.1). `AetherView` (`aether_view.tscn`, 2026-11-18): reusable presenter, an HDR `SubViewport` %World with its own world and glow `Environment` (local to scene), shown on %Screen through `aether_present.gdshader` at the window's real pixel size in the root's logical space; `apply_glow(look)`; no veil, no fade (the gallery moves onto it in a later PR). `AetherArrival` (`aether_arrival.tscn`, no `class_name`, 2026-11-18): the shipped route `&"aether"`, a stub destination (look `pale_marches_t1.tres`; %Backdrop, %Camera, %Lantern under the view's World); `on_route_entered` reads typed `aether_time_s` (float, 0), `tier` (int, 1), `camera_centre` (Vector2, (320, 180) ✱), `camera_zoom` (float, 1.0), puts the lantern at the camera centre, then `Camera2D.force_update_scroll()` and `AetherBackdrop.refresh()` so the first drawn frame is right; world time then runs on; no fade or tween on entry; `ui_cancel` does nothing yet; the flow after arrival is not designed (§11.1). Details: ART-001 §16.6. Not a dependency of `features/cabinet`; the cabinet reveal keeps its own `shaders/aether_fog.gdshader` | **built (gallery pass 1; `AetherView`, `AetherArrival` stub and route `&"aether"` 2026-11-18)**; `WarpComposer` applying `AetherLook` is planned |

Each empty folder has a `README.md` naming its owner doc. `core/audio/` holds `sfx_synth.gd` (fallback synth), `cue_policy.gd` + `cue_policy_entry.gd` (voice policy, data in `data/audio/cue_policy.tres`) and `cue_ids.gd` + `cue_id_entry.gd` (cue id table, `data/audio/cue_ids.tres`); tests in `core/audio/tests/`. Shared, feature-agnostic code lives in `core/` (`audio`, `components`, `data`, `i18n`, `math`, `physics`, `rng`, `routing`, `save`, `ui`, plus `automation` (dev/QA only, §9.1)). Menus and the bootstrap sector live in `scenes/`.

### 2.9a VectorText (`core/ui/vector_text/`)

The text engine for everything outside the Act 0 screen (decided in Q-T23, §11.2 row "Q-T23 resolved"; built 2026-11-18, §11.2). `PixelText` stays the face on the 256×304 screen (§1.3); `VectorText` draws at native resolution, sharp at any window size.

| Piece | Role |
|---|---|
| `VectorGlyphSet` (`Resource`) | glyph table: strokes in em units, advance, ink right edge, kerning, baseline, descent, line gap. Data: `data/ui/vector_glyphs.tres` (strokes stored as stroke strings) |
| `VectorFace` (`Resource`) | look at a size: `stroke_width`, `size` (cap height), `tracking` in logical px, plus cosmetic `jitter`, `grain`, `seed` (✱ engineering, no gameplay meaning; jitter and grain come from a local hash of `seed` only, never a gameplay or `warp` RNG stream). Data: `data/ui/vector_face.tres` |
| `VectorLayout` (`RefCounted`) | result: strokes in logical px, `size`, line metrics, line texts, `missing` characters |
| `VectorText` (static, no node) | `layout(text, glyphs, face, align, max_width, glyph_filter) -> VectorLayout` is pure and static; `measure(...)` gives the ink size; `build_mesh(layout, width)` tessellates |
| `VectorLabel` (`Control`) | the node: takes a `tr()` key (or `text` for dynamic text), `face`, `glyphs`, `color`, `alignment`, `wrap` / `max_width` |

- The two `.tres` files carry no `ds_namespace`: they are look data, not tunables, so they are not in the §3.4 registry.
- **Minimum stroke:** `MIN_PHYSICAL_STROKE` = 1.25 physical px ✱. A thinner stroke, especially near 45°, can cover no pixel centre and rasterise to nothing (the same failure as §1.3's drawing rule). `VectorLabel` widens the stroke at draw time from the canvas-to-screen scale; regression test `test_thin_diagonals_never_vanish`.
- **Glyph-lie seam:** `glyph_filter(ch, index) -> char | strokes | null` (a replacement character, replacement strokes in em units, or null to keep the glyph). WARP-001 §4.2 glyph lies plug in through it (via `HudLiar`); the Steady Ledger switches them off. Seam only: no caller yet.
- **`VectorLabel` behaviour:** the layout and the tessellated mesh are cached and rebuilt only when text, key, face, glyphs, size, alignment, filter or language changes. Round caps and joins; edges rely on the root's MSAA 2D (§1.2). Colour stays at or below 1.0, so text never blooms. A translucent colour double-blends where tessellated strokes overlap: fade by modulating the whole control, keep the colour alpha at 1. A character with no glyph draws a box and is listed in `VectorLayout.missing`.
- **Tests** (`core/ui/vector_text/tests/`): layout, wrap, alignment, minimum stroke, glyph filter, label caching, and locale glyph coverage (§7, L10N-001 §5).

### 2.10 Act 0 — the Cabinet (`features/cabinet/`)

Act 0 (GDD §6.1) is the in-world arcade machine (NARR-001 §5: the Cabinet). It is built as its own feature because ART-001 §7 makes it a **separate, peelable costume layer**: 1983-style vector art on a native-resolution bezel around a 256×304 portrait screen (WARP-001 W0-01), not the shader-rich generated parts of Act 1+ (ART-001 §14). Status: **built, five-stage shift** (P1, 2026-11-19: A0-1 to A0-5; A0-2 is restored as its own stage; PM ruling Q-T104, §11.1, §11.2). Stage counts: SYS-001 §1.6. The sub-bullets below that say "A0-3" for the last stage predate the shift: that stage is now `a0_5`.

- **The shift (P1, 2026-11-19; built in the arcade-layer P1 PR).** `CabinetStageRoster` (`features/cabinet/cabinet_stage_roster.gd`, `Resource`; `stages: Array[CabinetStage]`, `size()`, `get_stage(i)`, `index_of(id)`) is content, not tuning; `data/cabinet/stage_roster.tres` lists `a0_1` to `a0_5` in play order and `CabinetGame.roster` points at it (`CabinetGame.stages` is a read-only view). **Stage flow:** READY card → play → on clear or unmade, a mid-shift end (a0_1 to a0_4) shows only the banner (`CAB_VIGIL_COMPLETE` / `CAB_ENTITY_DOWN`) for `MID_SHIFT_HOLD_SECONDS` ✱ (= `CREDITS_SECONDS` × 0.6) with no credit roll (PM 2026-11-19, Q-T131), then `start_stage(next_stage_index())`; on the last stage (`is_last_stage()`) the full credits and the S7 service record roll (the first credits anyone sees), then THE PRETENCE. **Tally seam (P1b):** Sizzle's P3 tally card (`messages.show_tally`, agreed seam, not built) will replace the mid-shift banner; its content source is `CabinetGame.tally_lines(n, now, since)` (the `CAB_TALLY_TITLE` "VIGIL {n} TALLY" line, then the four stat lines for that stage only), with `stage_record_start` (the record at stage start) and `TALLY_STAT_INDICES` (positions of the stat lines). The service record and `ScoreKeeper` run across stages (not reset between them). `CabinetStage` gained `zone_key` (the zone line on the READY card, empty = none) and `zone_cue` (an AUDIO-001 cue id played under the card, empty = none; the renders are AUDIO-001 §12.16, PR #54). **Zone line:** `CabinetMessages` draws it above the READY title, `ZONE_GAP` 6 px ✱ between them (`ready_zone_top()`), `GAS_4`; the whole card ends `READY_CLEAR` 14 px ✱ above the play-view centre, where the Vigil spawns (Reviewer, PR #57). **Cues requested of Audio** (AUDIO-001 §12.16): `zone_drift`, `zone_censer_watch`, `zone_shoal`, `zone_stillwater` (renamed from `zone_hollow`, 2026-11-19; each ≤ `READY_SECONDS` 2.5 s) and `continue_taken`. The fourth zone is **Stillwater** (key `CAB_ZONE_STILLWATER`, "ENTERING STILLWATER"; PM 2026-11-19: "Hollow" clashed with the Hollow faction state and the Hollowed Composure band; Q-T106). **CONTINUE route contract:** GAME OVER routes to attract with `{"continue_stage": <int, 0-based>, "shift_total": <int>}` (`CabinetGame._game_over_params()`; the demo hands nothing). **Shift total (P1b, PM 2026-11-19, Q-T132):** `shift_total()` = the shift base + this credit's score; **run-scoped, never saved** (no profile key). A fresh start has base 0; on CONTINUE the base is the `shift_total` route param (`shift_total_of(params)`, a non-negative int or float, else 0). Attract passes both params back on CONTINUE (attract pass-through is Visual Design's follow-up, not built). The reveal routes `&"ruins"` with `{"shift_total": t}` for later Ember banking (the Ruins does not read it yet: not consumed yet; owner Act 1 / Ruins). Attract reads it and shows `CAB_CONTINUE` ("CONTINUE? STAGE {stage}", `{stage}` = index + 1 per L10N-001 §3, `CabinetGame.continue_line(i)`); START (a coin) routes to `cabinet` with the same `continue_stage`, and `on_route_entered` calls `new_game(i)` (`ScoreKeeper.reset()` for score 0, starting lives and the first extra-life threshold, `ServiceRecord.reset()`, `start_stage(i)`) and plays `continue_taken`. `continue_stage_of(params, count)` accepts an int or float inside `0..count-1`; anything else keeps stage 0. The offer lasts until the attract cycle reaches the demo. **Not built:** the attract side (the CONTINUE page and its param hand-off) belongs to Visual Design, pass 2b; until then GAME OVER's param is produced and the cabinet side is tested, but nothing draws or consumes it. The READY card's new keys `CAB_STAGE_A0_2` to `A0_5` (+ `_SUB`), `CAB_ZONE_*` (4) and `CAB_CONTINUE` are in `locale/en.po`; **furthest stage (P1b, Q-T133):** profile key `act0_stage_reached` (int; −1 = nothing to continue) is written at each stage start (`CabinetGame._write_stage_reached()`; a boundary, Act 0 is not "mid-run", §7.2) with save reason `&"cabinet_stage"`, kept at the maximum reached this shift, reset to −1 when a fresh shift opens (New Vigil, boot) and set to −1 at the reveal; never written in the demo. `CabinetGame.saved_continue_stage(profile, count)` returns that stage for attract's relaunch CONTINUE, or −1 when the key is missing or invalid, or when `act0_complete` is true; `CabinetStageRoster.default_size()` gives attract the stage count before a cabinet exists (Visual Design wires the relaunch CONTINUE after PR #57, not built); `CAB_STAGE_A0_3` (the old Last Normal Day) was renamed `CAB_STAGE_A0_5`. **P2 (built, see the next bullet; not playtested);** **P3 (not built):** Sizzle FX (intermission tally as a card or panel, never a full-field flood; reinforcement tell; ticker FX) and Visual Design's look (zone ink register, geode fill per its ART-001 ruling, unit scale); **attract expansion (not built, Q-T109):** a ~75 s cycle with a Shoal autopilot demo and a top-10 table.

- **Stage pressure (P2, 2026-11-19; PM, Q-T105 reopened for P2 only; built in the arcade-layer P2 branch).** Seven `CabinetStage` switches, each defaulting to the pre-P2 game (`a0_1` keeps all defaults; per-stage values and meaning: SYS-001 §1.6): `acolyte_reinforce_s` (0 = off), `acolyte_cap` (0), `geode_drift_s` (0 = off), `grubs_after_wake`, `grub_pilfer`, `acolyte_escort`, `carry_charges_in` (all false). No Vigil feel number changed. **Censer bells (`sim/acolyte_squad.gd`):** every `acolyte_reinforce_s` of sim time, while the Vigil is alive and under control (`vigil.alive and input_enabled`), if `living_count()` (guard ring included) is below `acolyte_cap`, the squad rings a bell (`world.sfx(&"censer_bell")`, AUDIO-001 §12.16) and, `reinforce_tell_s` later, `spawn()`s an ordinary Acolyte (ids, kill, score and S1/S4/S6 apply) at `reinforce_entry_px` outside the CURRENT view edge. Edge (`REINFORCE_EDGES`: down, up, right, left) and spot along it are rolled from `world.rng` at warning start; the point follows the camera. The cap is re-checked at arrival. **Clock rules:** timers run in the layer `tick(sdt)`: frozen in hit-stop, scaled by slow-mo; the interval clock stops while the Vigil is dead or not under control, but a bell already rung still delivers. **Read-only schedule API for Sizzle's P3 tell** (never writes): `reinforcement_pending()`, `reinforcement_time_left()` (clamped at 0, counts down), `reinforcement_entry()`, `reinforcement_edge()` (ZERO when none pending), `living_count()`, `REINFORCE_EDGES`. New field `Acolyte.guard` (joined at the Guard phase; guards never escort). **Geode drift-in:** every `geode_drift_s` while live geodes < floor(`stage.geodes` × `geode_drift_floor`), one geode appears off view and `geode_drift_clear_px` from the Vigil. **Grubs after the wake:** replacement Grubs keep being born; the entity eats them (+1 heal, as before). **Grub pilfer:** with `grub_pilfer`, a Grub prefers a loose shard within `grub_pilfer_radius` that the magnet is not pulling (`GlassField.in_magnet(s)`, `GlassField.pilferable(s)`), re-evaluated on the 0.4 s think; Grubs already sought the nearest unclaimed shard, so the effect is mild (Q-T134). **Escort:** non-guard, non-engaged Acolytes trail the nearest laden Grub at `escort_distance`, one per Grub, and do not mine while doing so. **Charge carry-over:** `CabinetGame.start_stage(i, carried := -1)`; `_end_charges` is captured when the stage clears or the entity is unmade; it is applied only on the credits to next-stage hand-off into a stage with `carry_charges_in`, capped at the magazine, and never on a new game, CONTINUE or `--stage`. **Extra lives:** `ScoreKeeper` awards at 10,000, 30,000, then every 40,000 (cap `lives_cap`), `next_extra_at()` returns the next threshold (Q-T107). No new signals (EVT-001 unchanged).

```
CabinetGame (Node, cabinet_game.gd)             stage flow only: READY → play → death/respawn/game over
│                                                → stage clear → credits → THE PRETENCE → route "ruins"
├─ Rig (ScreenRig, Control)                      640×360 reference units, scaled by k/s so bezel and screen share one transform (s = root canvas scale); `bezel_rect()` = the whole window in rig units; signal `refitted`; the end-of-Act 0 zoom scales it
│  ├─ ArcadeScreen (ArcadeScreen, SubViewportContainer)   material = shaders/crt.gdshader reading TEXTURE (scanline rows uniform = grid.y; resource_local_to_scene); forwards mouse in grid coords
│  │  ├─ Registers (VideoRegisters)                 the 16 colour registers (Williams video limits, layer one only); pushes `registers`, `match_rgb`, `match_reg`, `limits` to the parent's CRT material; see "Video registers" below
│  │  └─ ScreenViewport (SubViewport)               size = `CabinetScreenSpec.grid`, own 2D snap on, Nearest, MSAA off
│  │     ├─ World (ArcadeWorld)                     torus field, camera, shake, shared services; ticks layers in order:
│  │     │   Stars · Geodes · Glass · Ritual · Grubs · Acolytes · Shots · Vigil · Fx   (each an ArcadeLayer)
│  │     ├─ Autopilot (CabinetAutopilot)            attract demo (W0-03), captures, the end-to-end test
│  │     ├─ MawLayer/Maw (MawView)                  the hole that opens in the reveal; the generated "true body" assembles inside it
│  │     ├─ HudLayer/ScreenHud (CabinetScreenHud)   the 40 px HUD strip at the top of the grid (`CabinetScreenSpec.strip_rect()`): see "HUD strip" below
│  │     └─ MessageLayer/Messages (CabinetMessages) READY card, banners, captions, credits, the entity's big type (CRT-shaded)
│  │     (ArcadeScreen positions itself at the hole; World view, Stars, Maw and Messages are sized from the spec's play view, never from 640/360)
│  └─ Hud (CabinetHud)                              the printed bezel, after the screen under Rig: Field (BezelField: navy print field + key-black tube-mask edge around the hole, out to the window edges), BezelPrint ×2 (left, right column) drawing BezelArt pieces (see below); no readout windows
├─ Reveal (PretenceReveal)                          the scripted W0-07/W0-08 sequence (skippable once seen); tweens ArcadeScreen.material
└─ Overlays (CanvasLayer 10, ScreenStack)           pause/settings on the root viewport, native res, over the whole cabinet
```

`CrtLayer` and `BezelLayer` are gone: the CRT is a material on the screen's own texture, so it cannot touch the bezel and no layer ordering is needed. Rule ✱: no focusable Control inside `ScreenViewport` (each Viewport owns its focus); `ArcadeScreen` ignores forwarded input while the tree is paused. Rule ✱: no screen-side script holds a 640 or 360 literal; sizes come from `CabinetScreenSpec` (`test_screen_side_sizes.gd` greps the screen-side files for it). `HoleFit` (`features/cabinet/hole_fit.gd`) confines root-level screen content (outside the SubViewport) to the hole or the play view. `CabinetMessages` clips to the play view and wraps long lines with `PixelText.wrap`. Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_arcade_screen.gd`, `core/ui/tests/test_screen_fit.gd`, `features/cabinet/tests/test_screen_side_sizes.gd`, `tests/test_project_display.gd`.

**Video registers (Williams video limits, built 2026-11-18; layer one only).** `VideoRegisters` (`features/cabinet/video_registers.gd`) is a child of the node whose material is the CRT shader (the `ArcadeScreen` of the cabinet and of the attract scene alike) and pushes uniforms to it: `registers[16]` (current colours), `match_rgb[48]` and `match_reg[48]` (the match set: the 32 `Look.NEON` colours tagged with their designated register from `ScreenRegisters.pal_map`, then the 16 register defaults), and `limits` = 1 − `Look.t`. `crt_quantize` in `shaders/crt_common.gdshaderinc` assigns each screen pixel the register of the nearest match-set entry, then shows that register's **current** colour, so a register write recolours every pixel on it at once with no redraw; alpha blends snap to the nearest register. `crt.gdshader` quantizes before `crt_finish` (the attract-only `crt_attract.gdshader` is retired, Q-T80). Only what those materials shade is limited: the bezel, coin slot and anything at native resolution never are, and `Look.NEON` is unchanged (the bezel uses it). API: `write`, `read`, `reset`, `cycle(reg, colors, frames_per_step)`, `stop_cycle`, `is_cycling`, `advance(delta)`, `frame`. Cycling steps on 16.64 ms video frames counted from accumulated delta, not physics ticks. Register 15 is the cycling register; `cycle()` has no caller yet (no tuning value for a flash period exists). Values (defaults, `pal_map`, `video_frame_s`) are in `data/cabinet/screen_registers.tres` (`ScreenRegisters`, `ds_cabinet.registers`, §3.4); the table is in ART-001 §15.2a. Test: `features/cabinet/tests/test_screen_registers.gd`.

| Layer | Owns (rules) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `GeodeField` | drift; calm mining (a face per `face_hits`, one shard each, calm total = 3 + 1); over-fire detonation (7 hits / 1.2 s → 4–6 shards total, scattered) | SYS-001 §1.3 |
| `GlassField` | shards, magnet (no drag inside the magnet radius, matching M1, 2026-11-18), Charge pips (overflow glass stays), homing Null-Iron Charges, interception by Grubs/Acolytes/geodes | SYS-001 §1.4 |
| `SepulchreRitual` | deposits → segments (3 shards each), Guard/Quickening, Awakening (silence → "IT WAKES." → 3 s stagger window), rampage (contact death, eats to heal), 13 Charges to unmake, module knock-off before waking, Cold Sepulchre | SYS-001 §2, §2.1 |
| `GrubFlock` | claim → carry → deposit; flee the lamp unless carrying; keening on a lost deposit; rebirth from the Sepulchre | SYS-001 §3 |
| `AcolyteSquad` | hold range, censer shots on a 1.8 s cycle with a 21-frame tell, turret lag 0.25 s, mining at 50 %, Guard reinforcements | SYS-001 §3 |
| `ShotLayer` | lance bolts and censer shots, swept in ≤ 4 px steps (no tunnelling), capped at SYS-001 §9's 180 | SYS-001 §1.3, §9 |
| `VigilShip` | the Vigil in costume: **reuses `VigilMotor.step_velocity`** with SYS-001 §1.1 numbers, one-hit death, Surgelight, respawn shield | SYS-001 §1.1, §9 |
| `ScoreKeeper` | the 1983 score table, extra Vigil at 10,000 then every 20,000, cap 5 | SYS-001 §7.1, GDD §9.4 |

**Physics decision (2026-11-18):** the Cabinet does **not** use Godot physics bodies. Its field is a torus (GDD §4.2), drawn by every layer through `ArcadeWorld.to_screen()` with the short-way-round delta (`core/math/torus.gd`). Circle overlaps in torus space are exact, deterministic and cheap at Act 0 counts. Act 1+ sectors keep the §2.6 component architecture (CharacterBody2D, Area2D hurt/hitboxes).

**Boot-up and attract (owner request 2026-11-18).** `Boot` routes the first attract of a launch with params `{"power_on": true}` (not under `--smoke`); later visits pass nothing.

```
AttractMode (scenes/attract/)                   on_route_entered(params) handles power_on and continue_stage
├─ Rig (ScreenRig)                              same fit as the cabinet (hole + bezel_rect)
│  ├─ ArcadeScreen (SubViewportContainer)       the same node type, material (crt.gdshader) and 256×304 SubViewport as the game (Q-T80); the screen is drawn at grid scale inside it
│  │  ├─ Registers (VideoRegisters)             same register bank as the cabinet; target = ArcadeScreen's material
│  │  └─ ScreenViewport
│  │     ├─ Backdrop                            starfield behind the menu
│  │     ├─ StripHud                            the 40 px strip
│  │     ├─ Play/Center/Menu                    Logo (attract_logo.gd, 4× PixelText; marquee; blink INSERT CREDIT → PRESS NEW VIGIL once a credit is in), Pages (AttractPages), Omen (no buttons here any more)
│  │     └─ PowerOn (PowerOnTest)               CRT warm-up line, scrolling garbage-RAM noise, top-down wipe, self-test lines (RAM/ROM/LAMP/GLASS … OK, SELF TEST COMPLETE); noise frozen under photosensitivity or reduced motion; scrolls, never full-field flashes
│  ├─ Frame (AttractFrame)                      printed bezel via `BezelAssembly` with Field (BezelField); its `ScreenLight` samples the ScreenViewport only
│  ├─ ControlPanel (ControlPanel)               the strip of the bezel under the screen (Q-T34): NewVigil, Settings, Quit (ArcadeButton)
│  └─ CoinSlot (features/cabinet/coin_slot.gd)  focusable Control on the right bezel (moves with the right column); its coin prompt draws on the print, right of the door
└─ Overlays (CanvasLayer)                       pause/settings, native res
```
Pages: 4 cards under chasing marquee bulbs: PITCH (canon NARR Cabinet attract lines), HOW TO PLAY (live bindings per device), THE MISSION, SCORE TABLE (read from CabinetTuning). The Q-T34 text-scale hide is removed: the cards fit at 200 % text.

**Attract on the arcade SubViewport (Q-T80 resolved 2026-11-19, PM ruling with owner authority, option (a)).** The attract screen content is drawn inside the same `ArcadeScreen` SubViewport as the game, through the same `crt.gdshader` and `VideoRegisters`, so the register limits hold in attract too. `ScreenLight` (§2.10 pass 2) samples that SubViewport only; the full-window readback is gone. `AttractCrt` (`attract_crt.gd`) and `shaders/crt_attract.gdshader` are deleted. The viewport owns its own input, which the bezel's controls never see, so while `PowerOnTest` runs, `AttractMode._input` hands it any key, button or click, which skips the test and is consumed before the coin door or a pushbutton can act. Tests: `scenes/tests/test_attract_coin_slot.gd` (power-on start, skip, self-finish); a dedicated test that input is consumed inside the screen is not in the tree.

**The control panel (Q-T34 resolved 2026-11-19, PM ruling).** The attract menu is a physical control panel on the bezel strip under the screen. `CabinetBezel.control_panel()` is the strip and `panel_slot(i)` each button's slot; `ControlPanel` (`control_panel.gd`) only places its `ArcadeButton` children into their slots. `ArcadeButton` (`arcade_button.gd`) **extends `BaseButton`** (so `core/automation` recognises it, and `pressed`, `disabled`, focus and `ui_accept` are the engine's own); drawn by `shaders/cabinet/arcade_button.gdshader` as a translucent cap, lamp and chrome ring, with its label printed under it in `VectorText` from the existing keys (`MENU_NEW_VIGIL`, `MENU_SETTINGS`, `MENU_QUIT`). Kinds: NEW VIGIL is a big lit start button, dark before a credit and blinking once a credit is in (steady under `photosensitivity` or `reduced_motion`); SETTINGS and QUIT are smoked service caps, unlit. **One focus domain** with the coin door: up/down loop [NewVigil if credited] → Settings → Quit → CoinSlot → wrap; left/right walk the row and leave it for the coin door; default focus stays the coin door until a credit, then New Vigil; `ui_cancel` is unchanged (UI-001). **In game** the same panel (`ControlPanel.add_decorative`, added by `CabinetHud`) is decoration: lamps dark, no focus, mouse and input ignored, and it moves away with the peel. Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_control_panel.gd`, `test_decorative_panel.gd`, `scenes/tests/test_attract_mode.gd`.

**CONTINUE offer (Q-T104, PM ruling 2026-11-19, agreed with the Arcade layer session, which owns the cabinet side).** Route param `continue_stage` (int, 0-based; absent or −1 means no offer) on routes `&"attract"` and `&"cabinet"` (§2.1). Attract shows a CONTINUE page on the arcade screen in place of the cycling cards (`AttractPages.continue_stage`, `PixelText`, key `CAB_CONTINUE` formatted with stage + 1; steady under `reduced_motion` or `photosensitivity`). While the offer stands, New Vigil continues: it routes to `&"cabinet"` with `continue_stage` set (and, since P1b, the `shift_total` the GAME OVER route carried; the attract pass-through is Visual Design's follow-up, §2.10). The offer clears when attract reaches its idle demo (`clear_continue_offer()`). Built here: the attract side only; the cabinet's reading of `continue_stage` and its GAME OVER routing belong to the Arcade layer session, not verified in this tree. Tests: `scenes/tests/test_attract_mode.gd`.

- `CoinSlot` phases: TAPED → FALLING → OPEN → DROPPING → CREDITED; `spend()` returns to OPEN. Signals `tape_peeled`, `credit_inserted` (EVT-001). After `tape_hint_delay` a prompt says how to remove the tape (mouse: drag it off; pad/keys: hold accept while the coin door has focus), then how to insert a coin (click the slit / accept).
- New Vigil is dark (lamp off, disabled) and out of the focus loop until a credit is in; one credit max; New Vigil spends it. Default focus is the CoinSlot until credited; loop = [New Vigil if credited] → Settings → Quit → CoinSlot (the control panel, above).
- No idle demo while a credit is waiting or a peel gesture is in progress; otherwise W0-03's 30 s idle demo is unchanged.
- Tape removal is saved forever: profile key `cabinet_tape_peeled`, save reason `cabinet_tape` (§7.2), written in attract, never mid-run.
- **The printed bezel** (design: ART-001 §12.7a; decision §11.2, 2026-11-18). `CabinetBezel` (`cabinet_bezel.gd`) holds the layout constants (`SIDE`, `COIN_PLATE`, `slit()`, `coin_plate()`), shared text helpers and the coin-door geometry (the door is drawn by `CoinDoorFace`, see "Bezel realism" below); the old `draw_panel` and the readout windows are gone (PR B): the columns sit flush to the hole and the coin plate moves with the right column. `BezelField` (`bezel_field.gd`) paints the navy print field and the key-black tube-mask edge around the hole, out to the window edges (`ScreenFit.bezel_rect`), so field, columns and hole tile the window at every aspect. `BezelArt.build_left/build_right` (`bezel_art.gd`) return a deterministic list of `BezelPiece` (`bezel_piece.gd`: id, column, points, ink, z, pivot, kind) with hand-placed shapes and an integer-hash scatter (no RNG). One `BezelPrint` (`bezel_print.gd`) per column draws its pieces once and redraws only on a crack or ink change; the peel moves it by `position.x`. Inks are data: `BezelInks` (`bezel_inks.gd`, `ds_cabinet.bezel`, §3.4) in `data/cabinet/bezel_inks.tres`. `CabinetHud` (same public API; `MARGIN`, `READOUT_W`) draws under `Rig` after `ArcadeScreen`, and `AttractFrame` shares the bezel; the CRT is the screen's material, so it never warps the bezel (the old BezelLayer-above-CrtLayer ordering is retired, §11.2 2026-11-18). The attract scene's own screen-texture CRT (`crt_attract.gdshader`) is retired: attract now renders in the arcade SubViewport (Q-T80, above). **Superseded in part by PR C pass 2 (2026-11-19, below):** the field-plus-columns layout became one printed sheet with `VectorText`; the control-panel buttons are built (Q-T34, above). Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_bezel_art.gd` (field, columns and hole tile the window at all 8 test sizes), `test_cabinet_hud_layout.gd`.
- **Bezel realism (PR C pass 1, built 2026-11-18; design ART-001 §12.7a).** The bezel is a stack of real-object nodes, the same in attract (`AttractFrame`) and in game (`CabinetHud`), all native resolution, outside the screen CRT and registers. Order under `Rig`: `BezelField` and two `BezelPrint` columns (print material), `CoinDoorFace` (in game a child of `CabinetHud`; in attract of `CoinSlot`), then `BezelGlass` above the print. Values for every part come from `CabinetSurface` (`ds_cabinet.surface`, §3.4); this section names the nodes, not the numbers.
  - `CabinetLighting` (`cabinet_lighting.gd`, static): `apply(mat, surface)` pushes the **one cabinet light** into any part's `ShaderMaterial` (the uniforms of `shaders/cabinet/cabinet_material.gdshaderinc`): a warm key from the screen hole's side (the lit screen), a cool dim room fill, top-to-bottom falloff; `print_material(surface, origin, mottled)` builds the print material for canvas-drawn ink. Every physical part calls it, so they share one light.
  - `CabinetSurface` (`TunableResource`, `CabinetSurface.default()` loads the `.tres`), all ✱.
  - `BezelField` and `BezelPrint` use the print material (`shaders/cabinet/bezel_print.gdshader`): flat spot inks printed in reverse under glass over a white backer, with fade, mottle, halftone where the tone shifts, scuffs and flakes to the backer; `BezelArt` also applies a per-plate misregistration (deterministic, key-black plate is the reference).
  - `CoinDoorFace` (`coin_door_face.gd`, `ColorRect`, `shaders/cabinet/coin_door.gdshader`): the coin door as SDF shapes with height-field lighting: satin black steel, zinc parts, one bright lip at the slot, the lit price plate (which is the reject button), a recessed return pocket with flap, an offset lock, imperfect screws, causal wear. `glow` 0..1 is the lamp behind the lit plate, which spills red-orange light on its neighbours. It covers `CabinetBezel.coin_plate()` (or `fit_to(rect)`), draws behind its parent, and owns a `contact_shadow.gdshader` child (shadow that falls away from the screen glow plus a tight occlusion ring). The old `draw_coin_plate` is removed.
  - `CabinetBezel` coin-door API: `price_button(plate)`, `button_center()`, `button_half()` and `fits_price()` describe the **lit price plate**; `slit(plate)` is exactly the drawn slot opening; `draw_price` prints the price on the plate.
  - `BezelGlass` (`bezel_glass.gd`, `bezel_glass.gdshader`): sheen, dust, fingerprints and scratches over the field and both columns, never the screen hole (it paints only `BezelField.glass_rects(area, peel)`), with the door cut out; follows the peel and the window area.
  - `BezelVent` (`bezel_vent.gd`, `bezel_vent.gdshader`): perforated black-metal grilles at the top and bottom of each column and an apron vent under the door (replaces the white vent plates). `BezelSticker` (`bezel_sticker.gd`, `bezel_sticker.gdshader`): the NEW! slogan as a worn vinyl sticker with a lifted corner (replaces the starburst). The door backing's brass hinge blocks are gone.
  - Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_coin_door_face.gd` (surface keys and values, door covers the plate, slit is the drawn slot, price fits the lit plate, contact shadow, lighting from the hole), `test_bezel_glass.gd` (glass never covers the hole and tiles the window, follows the peel, vents and sticker exist, misregistration deterministic, field print material). Run results are in the PR report, not asserted here.
  - **Bezel realism pass 2 (PR C, built 2026-11-19; design ART-001 §12.7a "Built (pass 2)").** The bezel is now **one printed sheet**: full-bleed art, the big logo in the side-panel art, the how-to printed on the sheet, and a printed border round a rounded opening. The `BezelPiece` list, peel and crack seam are kept. `BezelAssembly` (`bezel_assembly.gd`, static) builds the shared parts for attract (`AttractFrame`) and the in-game `CabinetHud` in one draw order: room, columns, marquee, border, T-molding, glass, tube. New nodes (values all from `CabinetSurface`, §3.4; shaders in `shaders/README.md`):
    - `CabinetRoom` (`cabinet_room.gd`): the dark room beyond the sheet. `BezelMarquee` (`bezel_marquee.gd`): the backlit top sign (Q-T32 (a), top 28 units). `BezelFrame` (`bezel_frame.gd`): the printed border round the rounded opening. `CabinetTrim` (`cabinet_trim.gd`): T-molding and zinc bolts. `BezelText` (`bezel_text.gd`): `VectorText` for **all** bezel lettering; `PixelText` stays only inside the screen.
    - `ScreenLight` (`screen_light.gd`): read-only. Every `spill_interval` it averages a strided sample of what the tube shows (the arcade `SubViewport`, 256×304 texels, in game and in attract alike; the old full-window readback in attract is gone, Q-T80 resolved), eases the result, and writes the global shader uniform `cab_screen_light` (via `CabinetLighting.set_screen_light`, §1.3). It never writes into the screen or a viewport. With `photosensitivity` on it holds a fixed steady value. Not sampled headless.
    - `ScreenTube` (`screen_tube.gd`, a `ColorRect`): an overlay drawn **after** the screen: a rounded tube-mask opening plus a very faint glass layer (reflection, dust, bloom bleed, inner shadow). The glass alpha is clamped by `tube_max_shift` (`ScreenTube.max_alpha`), so no screen channel moves more than that. The CRT and the register quantize are untouched. Off switch for the glass (and the spill, above): the existing `photosensitivity` setting; no separate glow setting exists. The mask itself replaces the corner texels (§11.2 2026-11-19).
    - **Tone-polygon convention:** a `BezelPiece`'s alpha is its tone; the print shader halftones it per plate (rotated grids, dot gain).
    - Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_bezel_pass2.gd`, `test_bezel_art.gd`. Run results are in the PR report, not asserted here.
  - **Built (pass 2b, 2026-11-19; design ART-001 §12.7a "Built (pass 2b polish)"):** the control panel (Q-T34, see "Boot-up and attract") and the polish commit 520d874: the glass reads through light (`BezelGlass`), UV fade and grime in `bezel_print.gdshader`, the marquee's hot band, shadow and spill (`BezelMarquee`), the flat door label and dimmed slot lip (`coin_door.gdshader`), and a faint screen-coloured spill in `CabinetRoom`. All values in `CabinetSurface` (§3.4).
  - **Not built:** the peel/crack/decay zoom (Q-T20).
- **HUD strip** (`CabinetScreenHud`, `cabinet_screen_hud.gd`, 40 px, all live readouts; the layout is code in that file, the numbers here are the owner's pick from the mock, 2026-11-18): left, 1UP, the score at 2× PixelText when it fits 80 px (else 1×), and the Vigil icons; centre, the Ledger Compass at `Rect2(88,3,80,31)`; right, HI and its value, 5 magazine lamps ending at x 248, and the SEPULCHRE value (`segments/total`, or AWAKE; hidden once unmade) right-aligned ending at x 202; a 1 px separator at y 39. The `eaten` thresholds are unchanged; `peel` no longer moves the readouts. The blinking INSERT CREDIT shows only in the attract demo (`hud.demo`) (ART-004 §8.6, 2026-11-18). Locale keys `CAB_VIGILS`, `CAB_GLASS`, `CAB_SEPULCHRE` were removed.
- `InputLabels` (`core/ui/input_labels.gd`) names bindings per device from the live `InputMap`; `is_accept()` adds a pad-A fallback (§11.1 Q-T19).
- `CabinetBootTuning` (`ds_cabinet.boot`, §3.4) holds all ✱ presentation timings.
- **FX tuning and hit-stop (S0/S1, 2026-11-18).** `CabinetFxTuning` (`features/cabinet/cabinet_fx_tuning.gd`, `data/cabinet/cabinet_fx_tuning.tres`, `ds_cabinet.fx`, §3.4) holds every FX-event number (shake, burst, ring); `ArcadeWorld` takes it as `@export fx_tuning`. The SYS-001 §9 shake caps (4 px, 0.25 s) remain 🔒 literals in `ArcadeWorld.shake()`. `ArcadeWorld.hitstop(frames, beat)` freezes the **sim** in `ArcadeWorld.step` for `hitstop_left` frames (repeat calls extend, never shorten); it is not `Engine.time_scale` or tree pause, so audio, input, `CabinetGame` state timers and the CRT clock keep running. `ArcadeWorld.shader_time` is that CRT clock (fed to the CRT `time_s`) and does not freeze. Beats: `charge_hits_entity`, `entity_unmade`, `vigil_death`, `it_wakes`, `modules_knocked`. Reduced Motion multiplies the frames by `reduced_motion_scale` (0 disables). Signal `hitstop_started(beat)` (EVT-001), consumed by the audio chat (AudioService cues). Values owner-approved 2026-11-18, SYS-001 §9 game-feel rows (Q-T44 resolved). Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_cabinet_fx_tuning.gd`, `test_cabinet_hitstop.gd`.
- **Service record and the credits stat lines (S7, 2026-11-18).** `ServiceRecord` (`features/cabinet/service_record.gd`, `RefCounted`) holds run-wide in-memory counters: `grubs_destroyed` (player lance kills only), `glass_mined` (shards freed by the player's mining or detonation; not Acolyte mining, not the entity devouring), `charges_thrown` (successful launches), `glass_carried` (shards Grubs deposit at the Sepulchre). `CabinetGame` owns it (same lifetime as `ScoreKeeper`; never saved; not reset between stage 1 and stage 2) and hands it to `ArcadeWorld` as `record`, which survives `start()`. The stage-2 full credits roll (after the entity is unmade) inserts four true stat lines after `CAB_CREDITS_3`: `CAB_CREDITS_STAT_GRUBS`, `_MINED`, `_CHARGES`, `_CARRIED` (`"GRUBS DESTROYED: %d"` etc., `locale/en.po`); mid-shift stage ends no longer roll credits (P1b, §2.10), so only the last stage's roll carries stats. `CabinetMessages.roll_credits(lines, stat_indices)` emits `credits_stat_shown(index)` once per stat line as it scrolls into view (EVT-001); `CabinetGame` plays the AudioService cue `tally_blip` (cue owned by AUDIO-001). **Owner-approved 2026-11-18 (Q-T45 resolved):** the four wordings, recorded with the whole credits set in NARR-001 §7.5, and the `CabinetFxTuning` fields `credits_scroll_px_per_s` 24.0 (the old literal) and `credits_full_seconds` 13.0 (was 9.0; four extra lines would push the last line off screen; measured last line top at y=208 of 264 at roll end), now SYS-001 §9 rows (⚙). Lines state counts only (NARR-001 §11.10).
- **Last-Charge slow-mo (S3, 2026-11-18).** When the Charge whose arrival brings the entity's `hits_left` to 0 is within `slowmo_trigger_px` of the entity, the Act 0 **sim** slows: `ArcadeWorld.sim_scale` ramps (in real time) to `slowmo_scale`. `ArcadeWorld.step` computes `sdt = delta * sim_scale` for every layer except the Vigil layer, which runs on real `delta` so the player keeps full control; `time` and shake decay use `sdt`; `shader_time` (CRT), audio, input and `CabinetGame` state timers stay real. It is not `Engine.time_scale` and not tree pause. Charge age ticks on `sdt`, so a Charge lives slightly longer in real time during slow-mo. Lethal detection is re-evaluated every tick: the lethal Charge is the `hits_left`-th nearest alive Charge by torus distance; slow-mo ends on interception, gutter or entity heal. When the Charge lands, `end_slowmo()` snaps the scale to exactly 1.0 before hit-stop starts. Reduced Motion: no slow-mo. Failsafe `slowmo_max_real_s`, then a lockout until the condition clears. Signal `ArcadeWorld.slowmo_changed(scale: float)` (EVT-001) fires whenever `sim_scale` changes, including exactly 1.0 at the end; `CabinetGame` connects it to `AudioService.set_bed_pitch_scale(scale)` (AUDIO-001 §12.11; the method clamps 0.25..1.0) and resets it to 1.0 on exit and on stage start. Values are ⚙ owner-approved 2026-11-18 and live in SYS-001 §9 (`CabinetFxTuning` group "slow-mo"; §11.1 Q-T46 resolved). Trigger distance `slowmo_trigger_px` 130.0 (owner: "start further out", 2026-11-18). Measured on autopilot stage 2, seeds 9/11/13: real-time slow-mo in the last episode 0.833 to 0.850 s (trigger sweep: 80 px 0.23 s; 120 px 0.70-0.77 s; 125 px 0.77-0.83 s; 130 px 0.83-0.85 s; 140 px 0.92-0.98 s; 145 px and up exceeds 1.0 s; plateau 1.35-1.45 s from 200 px where Charge flight limits it). Minimum scale 0.25 and scale exactly 1.0 when `entity_unmade` fired were measured in an earlier run at the old 80 px trigger. Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_cabinet_slowmo.gd` (17 tests).
- **Lance bite (S4, 2026-11-18).** Cabinet sim only; no new signals. (1) **Muzzle star:** a 1-drawn-frame `GAS_5` plus at the muzzle when the Vigil fires; suppressed under `photosensitivity` and under the accessibility setting `muzzle_flash` (default on; owner flash ruling 2026-11-18, AUDIO-001 §8.3: a few-pixel star is not a luminance flash). (2) **Recoil:** a visual-only draw offset of the ship opposite the aim (`recoil_px` for `recoil_frames`); it never touches position, velocity, facing or collision, consistent with SYS-001 §1.3 "no recoil" for the emitter; suppressed under `reduced_motion`. The muzzle and recoil counters count **drawn** frames (hit-stop skips the tick but the frame still redraws). (3) **Impact flecks:** `FxLayer.fleck` draws two 1 px specks perpendicular to the lance direction when a lance stops on a bite target (geode, Grub, Acolyte). (4) **No bite:** the old three random sparks at the Sepulchre/entity (`ritual_lance_burst_count`, `ritual_lance_burst_speed`, both now removed from `CabinetFxTuning`) are replaced by `FxLayer.chalk_tick(at, dir)`, a dim `BONE_2` dash perpendicular to the lance, plus the AudioService cue `lance_dull` via `world.sfx(&"lance_dull", lance_dull_db)` (cue owned by AUDIO-001 §12.11). `SepulchreRitual.lance_test(p, dir)` gained the `dir` parameter. `fleck` and `chalk_tick` are deterministic: fixed geometry, no `world.rng`. Fields in `CabinetFxTuning` group "lance bite", ⚙ owner-approved 2026-11-18 and SYS-001 §9 game-feel rows (Q-T50 resolved): `muzzle_star_px` 3, `muzzle_star_frames` 1, `recoil_px` 1, `recoil_frames` 2, `fleck_speed_px_s` 60.0, `fleck_life_s` 0.12, `tick_len_px` 3, `tick_life_s` 0.15, `lance_dull_db` 0.0. Tests: `features/cabinet/tests/test_lance_bite.gd` (9), `test_the_lance_does_not_bite_the_entity` (extended), `test_cabinet_fx_tuning.gd`. **M1 counterpart (not built):** `features/vigil` and `features/arc_lance` have none of this. The M1 chat later wires `ArcLanceEmitter.lance_fired` and `LanceProjectile.lance_hit`; neither signal carries a position or origin today, and classifying a non-biting target needs a hurtbox flag or group.
- **Readable death, "the coroner's chalk" (S6, 2026-11-18).** Cabinet only, no new signals. On Vigil death the GAS death burst plays first; the chalk starts only after it fades (delay = max(`death_chalk_delay_s`, the burst ring's life via `FxLayer.ring_life`)). All chalk is 1 px `BONE_4`, no alpha: (1) a SOLID closed outline of the Vigil's own hull (`VigilShip.hull_points`) at the death spot and facing, `death_outline_scale` × hull size, drawn on over `death_outline_draw_s`; (2) then ONE DASHED (2 on / 1 off) arrow, `death_arrow_px` (= `VigilShip.HULL_LENGTH` 15 × scale), from the killer's side pointing into the outline, drawn on over `death_arrow_draw_s`; (3) if the killer is farther than the arrow, a dashed tracking LEADER continues the shaft to the killer's live edge (killers of at least `death_big_killer_px` radius, a ✱ engineering cutoff approved by the PM, track `entity_pos` each draw, torus-aware), ending in a `death_edge_tick_px` tick when off-view; (4) **a shot death rings the SHOOTER, not the spent shot (2026-11-19, The Reviewer's ruling, PM approved).** One grammar for every death: solid = you; the dashed line ends on what killed you. `ShotLayer.Shot.shooter_id` (default -1); `ShotLayer.fire_censer(from, dir, shooter_id := -1)`; `AcolyteSquad` passes its stable `Acolyte.id` and offers `AcolyteSquad.find_by_id`; `AcolyteSquad.DRAWN_RADIUS` 11 ✱. Shooter alive and on view: a dashed leader from the outline edge facing it plus a small dashed ring around the shooter (drawn radius + `death_ring_pad_px`; coarse 3/3 dashes only when the ring is wider than the scaled hull), both tracking the live Acolyte through the DEAD state. Alive but off view: the leader to the edge tick. Dead or despawned, or id -1: the arrow only. The old ring around the consumed shot is removed. `FxLayer.death_killer_pos(m)` is the single live-killer resolver: `entity_pos` for the big killer, the live Acolyte by id for a shot, `Vector2.INF` when the shooter is gone or the id is -1 (`death_killer_gone`). Held until respawn; cleared on respawn and stage start. Reduced Motion: the delay stays (sequencing), then the chalk appears complete at once. API: `VigilShip.kill(killer_pos, from_dir, killer_radius, shooter_id := -1)` with defaults (no mark; `from_dir` points Vigil to killer); `FxLayer.DeathMark.shooter_id`; `FxLayer.death_mark`, `clear_death_marks`, `death_outline`, `death_outline_segments`, `death_arrow`, `death_leader`, `death_ring_segments` (1 px dashed rings as pixel-centred chords, §1.3), `death_killer_pos`, `death_edge_tick`, `death_chalk_delay`, `ring_life`. Dev-op note for automation: `shoot_vigil` fires with no shooter (-1), so `a0_acolyte_shot_death` and `a0_offview_killer` show the arrow only; to capture the shooter cases the dev op must pass the spawned Acolyte's id (automation owns this follow-up). ✱ constants in code: `FxLayer.DASH_RING_ON/OFF` 3/3, `DASH_SMALL_ON/OFF` 2/1, `SOLID_ON_PX`, `ARROW_HEAD_PX`, `ARROW_TIP_INSET_PX`, `MIN_RING_GAP_PX`; `SepulchreRitual.BODY_DRAWN_RADIUS` (`BODY_RADIUS` + 4). Grammar: solid = you, dashed = pointing. **Decision:** chalk means "what you lost / what killed you", rhyming with chalk as the tell for eaten HUD elements later (WARP-001 §4.1-§4.3). Palette: one active entry, `BONE_4` (white register); it never shares pixels with the GAS burst because it starts after the burst fades (16-register limit). Review: four rounds with The Reviewer; signed off in round 4, conditional on a closed solid triangle in the 2x capture. Values ⚙ PM-approved 2026-11-18 (owner authority), SYS-001 §9 (Q-T65 resolved); `death_big_killer_px` stays ✱. Chalk clips to the PLAYFIELD (`CabinetScreenSpec.playfield_rect()`: the play view minus the `MAKER_BAND_PX` ✱ 10 px band where the "© LANTERN OFFICE" line is drawn), so the edge tick and leader never cross cabinet text (Reviewer polish, 2026-11-19).
- **Surgelight afterimage and thrust wake (S5, 2026-11-18).** Cabinet only, deterministic (no `world.rng`), position and velocity unchanged, no new signals. A per-tick ring buffer of Vigil position and facing (frozen in hit-stop, cleared on respawn and reset, torus-relative) feeds `ghost_count` ghosts drawn as CORNER BRACKETS only (nose plus two tail vertices, never closed): each edge is drawn for `ghost_bracket_frac_1/2/3` of its length, shared nose edges capped at `GHOST_SHARED_EDGE_MAX` 0.4 per end so the nearest ghost stays open. Spacing grows with age: ghost k is `ghost_spacing_ticks` × k(k+1)/2 ticks back (3, 9, 18). Drawn during the surge and `ghost_hold_s` after; colours `GAS_3`, `GAS_2`, `GAS_1` (nearest to farthest). While thrusting, a dotted 1 px wake: `wake_dots` dots every `wake_spacing_ticks` ticks, `GAS_2` for the nearer half and `GAS_1` for the farther. Palette: three active entries (`GAS_3`, `GAS_2`, `GAS_1`; yellow and orange registers), no starfield entry shared. Review: signed off by The Reviewer in round 3. Capture: `tests/scenarios/sizzle_s5_surge.scenario.json` (§9.1; suite-eligible, screenshots skipped headless). Values ⚙ PM-approved 2026-11-18 (owner authority), SYS-001 §9 (Q-T65 resolved).
- **True-body frame (S2, 2026-11-18).** Cabinet only, deterministic, no new signals (EVT-001 unchanged). On each Charge hit on the costume entity, for `true_frame_frames` (⚙ 1, SYS-001 §9) **drawn** frames (the counter counts drawn frames, so it survives hit-stop), the costume is replaced by the entity's TRUE silhouette: a vertebral COLUMN on the shared S-curve (below), one filled `Pal.NULL` polygon (it occludes the stars) with a closed, pixel-centred 1 px `Pal.BONE_3` outline, 20 vertebrae as in-phase lobes on both edges (each vertebra bulges on both sides at the same point on the curve, pinched at the 19 gaps; a beaded, segmented column, after review: offset lobes read as a worm or lightning), head on the costume centre and overrunning it (`TRUE_LENGTH_MULT` 3.5 × `BODY_DRAWN_RADIUS`, ✱), trailing down or up (`true_frame_dir_for`). No eye, face, teeth or interior. The hit's score popup is moved just outside the column's box (`FxLayer.place_last_text`, `TRUE_POPUP_GAP` ✱). Registers: the same two entries as the costume's disc and plates (NULL, BONE_3), so it is a shape swap, not a flash; all flat, no alpha. **Rulings:** GDD §20 (closed 2026-11-17: the entity is seen in Act 0 only as a silhouette) and NARR-001 §3.4 (no face); PM ruling 2026-11-18 (silhouette only). **Review:** The Reviewer found the first radial ring read as a mouth, burst or gear, so the silhouette became a spine. **Shared curve:** `MawView.spine_points(n)` (unit space; asymmetric S, sway grows toward the tail, vertebra spacing tapers about 2:1), `spine_place`, `spine_silhouette`; ✱ `SPINE_*` constants in `maw_view.gd`. The reveal's own true-body drawing is unchanged (it shares only the vertebra-ring maths `vertebra_angle` / `vertebra_radius`). **Foreshadow idea:** one curve, drawn three times (the 1-frame hit silhouette, the star constellation, the reveal's vertebrae), so the player has already seen the shape when the Pretence names it. Test: `features/cabinet/tests/test_true_body_frame.gd`. `true_frame_frames` ⚙ PM-approved 2026-11-19 (owner authority), SYS-001 §9 (Q-T95 resolved); `TRUE_LENGTH_MULT` and `TRUE_POPUP_GAP` stay ✱ here.
- **Star spine (S8, 2026-11-18).** Stage A0-3 THE LAST NORMAL DAY only (`CabinetStage.spine_stars` true in `stage_last_normal_day.tres`; every other stage false). 40 extra starfield stars, a pair per vertebra straddling the shared curve at the S2 width profile (scaled), so the constellation draws the column's outline as a ladder (far parallax layer; pairs land side by side on their vertebra slot at the hand-off), `BONE_2` 1 px during play and never highlighted. When the Pretence reveal's pull begins they turn `BONE_5` 2x2 and hold while `Starfield.pull` < `spine_hold_pull` (⚙ 0.02, SYS-001 §9, about 1.1 s: the constellation forms one beat before the pull), then travel over `spine_pull_span` (⚙ 0.07, SYS-001 §9, arriving at pull 0.09, about 2.4 s, before `maw.reveal` starts at 2.5 s) to `MawView`'s vertebra slots. Arrived stars are drawn by `MawView` above its disc until vertebra i is revealed, at which point the star has become the vertebra. `Starfield.maw` and `MawView.stars` are plain references wired in `cabinet_game.gd` code (no signals, no new node paths). `pretence_reveal.gd` is untouched. Deterministic (no `world.rng`). Palette: `BONE_2` during play, `BONE_5` during the reveal; flat, no alpha. Test: `features/cabinet/tests/test_star_spine.gd`. `spine_hold_pull` and `spine_pull_span` ⚙ PM-approved 2026-11-19 (owner authority), SYS-001 §9 (Q-T95 resolved); the `SPINE_*` and `SPINE_PAIR_GAP` 2 constants stay ✱ here.
- Cues `power_on`, `tape_peel`, `coin`: AUDIO-001 §11.7. Glyph "·" added to `PixelText` for the canon line.

Data: `data/cabinet/cabinet_screen.tres` (`CabinetScreenSpec`, `ds_cabinet.screen.grid` ✱, Vector2i, 256×304; `hud_strip_height` ✱ 40; helpers `strip_rect`, `play_rect`, `play_size`, `play_centre`; §3.4); `data/cabinet/cabinet_tuning.tres` (`CabinetTuning`, `ds_cabinet.tuning.*`, every value cites SYS-001 or is ✱) and one `CabinetStage` per level (`stage_first_vigil.tres` a0_1, `stage_drift.tres` a0_2, `stage_censer_watch.tres` a0_3, `stage_shoal.tres` a0_4, `stage_last_normal_day.tres` a0_5; counts in SYS-001 §1.6) plus `stage_roster.tres` (`CabinetStageRoster`, play order), inside SYS-001 §4's Seam 1 T0 budget.

Art: everything is code-drawn from `Pal` (`core/ui/pal.gd`, checked against the palette file by a test). Text uses the **Cabinet face**, a 5 × 7 bitmap drawn by `PixelText` (`core/ui/pixel_text.gd`, ART-001 §11) at integer scales only (`PixelText` is the Act 0 screen face; `VectorText`, §2.9a, is the face everywhere else). There are no exceptions (D-13): the reveal's "true body" inside the maw is a richer generator (`MawView`), not a sprite.

Capture and test switches (after `--`): `--autopilot`, `--stage=N`, `--segments=N`, `--hits=N`, `--smoke-frames=N`, `--no-save` (SaveService keeps slots in memory). Example, film the reveal:
```
tools\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe --path . --write-movie out\f.png --fixed-fps 30 --quit-after 1500 -- --smoke --smoke-frames=99999 --autopilot --stage=1 --segments=20 --hits=2 --no-save
```

---

## 3. Data resources and `ds_` keys

### 3.1 Layout
`data/<feature>/<name>.tres` — loose `.tres` tunables, one `TunableResource` subclass each. The class script lives with its feature (`features/vigil/vigil_move_stats.gd`) or in `core/` when shared (`core/components/health_stats.gd`). Non-tunable data (route table, theme, bus layout) also lives in `data/` but is not a `TunableResource`.

Create a **Resource** when data is shared, tuned, or authored (stats, items, abilities, Sigils, kits, dialogue lines). Create a **scene** when it needs nodes, a transform, collision or a lifetime. Never put a game number in a script constant.

### 3.2 TunableRegistry
`core/data/tunable_registry.gd`. Not an autoload: `TunableRegistry.new().scan()` walks `res://data`, loads every `TunableResource`, and maps each `ds_` key → (resource, property). Gameplay uses the typed resource directly via `@export`; the registry exists for the lint, debug tooling and save validation.

### 3.3 Data lint
`core/data/tests/test_tunable_registry.gd` fails on: a namespace not starting `ds_`, a malformed key, a duplicate key, a missing required key, a non-positive movement value, `max_hp < 1`. The SYS-001 §10 ↔ key cross-check (every register row ↔ exactly one key) is **not** built: SYS-001 defines no `ds_` keys yet (§11 Q-T03).

### 3.4 Key convention
`ds_<feature>.<group>.<property>`, lower snake case, e.g. `ds_vigil.move.max_speed`. The `.tres` sets `ds_namespace` (`ds_vigil.move`) and `source_section` (the SYS-001 row it copies). Each property comment cites its SYS-001 row and lock state (🔒 / ⚙ / ✱). Sprite ids (`ds_<kit>_<part>…`, ART-002 §2) share the prefix but use underscores, never dots, so the two never collide.

| Key | Value source | Lock |
|---|---|---|

| `ds_vigil.move.drag`, `.brake`, `.snap_turn` | SYS-001 §1.1 | ⚙ |
| `ds_vigil.move.hitbox_circle_px` | SYS-001 §9 hitbox policy | 🔒 (reading open, §11 Q-T04) |
| `ds_vigil.hull.max_hp` | SYS-001 §1.2 Hull HP | 🔒 base 1; D-08 A/B by data edit |
| `ds_vigil.hull.invulnerable_after_hit_s` | no SYS-001 row; 0 | ✱ |
| `ds_arc_lance.{fire_rate_per_s, projectile_speed, visual_length_px, hits_per_projectile}` (`ArcLanceStats`, `data/arc_lance/arc_lance.tres`) | SYS-001 §1.3 | 🔒 fire rate base; others ⚙ |
| `ds_arc_lance.damage`, `.lifetime_s` | SYS-001 §1.3 (derived rows, approved by owner 2026-11-18) | ✱ |

**D-08 runtime API (M1 slice 1).** `Vigil.set_hull_max_hp(int)` / `get_hull_max_hp()` switch the hull at runtime; they call `HealthComponent.set_max_hp`, which duplicates the stats resource (the shared `.tres` is never mutated). Post-hit grace (R-11) applies only when `max_hp >= 2`.

**D-08 launch switch (M1 slice 2, part A).** `HullMode` (`features/vigil/hull_mode.gd`) reads `--hull=1|2` from the command line; an absent or invalid value falls back to the data value `ds_vigil.hull.max_hp` (1). `Vigil._ready` applies it through `apply_hull_override`. `HullMode` has its own small `--key=` parser because `CabinetGame._arg_int` is private to the Cabinet. A persisted setting is deferred: it needs a `SettingsService` `DEFAULTS` key and a settings-screen row with `tr()` keys. `--hull` is honoured in release exports too (no build gate); a known A/B switch to remove or gate once D-08 is decided (PM, 2026-11-19).

| `ds_composure.{min_value, max_value, start_value, clear_floor, frayed_floor, gaze_cone_per_s, gaze_drain_per_s, gaze_drain_min_tier, chant_radius_per_s, warp_passive_per_s_per_tier, charge_lost, charge_spent, death, death_recover_s, surgelight_use, surgelight_min_tier, surgelight_max_per_s, shrine_destroyed, matron_killed, procession_let_pass, darkness_delay_s, darkness_per_s, darkness_cap, clear_eyed}` (`ComposureStats`, `data/composure/composure.tres`): 0, 10, 10, 7, 4, −1, −0.5, 3, −0.5, −0.1, −0.5, 0, −2, 60, −1, 2, 2, +1, +2, +2, 5, 0.5, 2, −1 | SYS-001 §1.5 (rows and bands; start 10 = Q-T118). Band floors read the integer ranges as Clear ≥ 7, Frayed ≥ 4, Unmoored > 0, Hollowed = 0 for fractional values | per SYS-001 §1.5 (🔒 most rows; ✱ `charge_spent`, the Surgelight row) |
| `ds_composure.flame.{base_x_px, half_width_px, steady_length_px, flicker_length_px, gutter_length_px, hollow_length_px, flicker_hz, flicker_swing_px, gutter_hz, gutter_swing_px, max_motion_hz}` (`CockpitFlameStats`, `data/composure/cockpit_flame.tres`): −3, 2, 6, 6, 3, 6, 2.0, 1.5, 1.0, 1.0, 3.0 | WARP-001 §4.1 cockpit flame, §4.2 L10; motion capped at 3 Hz (ART-001 §12.9); engineering only, no SYS-001 row | ✱ |
| `ds_hud.wick.{clear_height_px, frayed_height_px, unmoored_height_px, hollowed_height_px, flame_half_width_px}` (`ComposureWickStats`, `data/hud/composure_wick.tres`): 11, 8, 5, 3 (hollow), 3 | ART-001 §12.5 wick (64 × 16); Q-T119 four band steps; engineering only, no SYS-001 row | ✱ |
