## 4. Gameplay runtime

Status for all of §4: **designed, not built** (except where a row says otherwise). This section is the contract the first gameplay features are built against.

### 4.1 The tick
The simulation is the 60 Hz physics tick (§1.2). Every gameplay state change happens in `_physics_process`; `_process` only draws. Order inside one tick is fixed by `process_physics_priority` ✱:

| Priority | Stage | Owner | Reads | Writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| −20 | Clock and run timers | `RunSession` (§4.2) | wall tick | Hourglass Clock, Seam timers |
| −10 | Intent | `VigilInput`, Choir brains | devices / AI state | intent fields only |
| 0 | Motors | `VigilMotor`, unit motors; built M1 slice 3 PR 4 (default priority 0): `OublietteGrub` (state machine + steering), `NullIronCharge` (homing flight), `ChargeLauncher` (consumes the deploy latch), `GrubProcession` (respawn timers), `Sepulchre` | intent, stats, `ShardField` hooks | velocity, position (`move_and_slide`), Charge outcomes, deposits |
| 1 | Geode imaging (M1 sandbox) | `GeodeSandbox` (built, M1 slice 3 PR 3; the priority is set by the sector) | the Vigil's wrapped position (after `SectorWrap` in the same tick) | each geode node's position = the image of its torus home nearest the Vigil |
| 10 | Shots | `ShotSweeper` (§4.6) | shot pool | swept hits queued |
| 20 | Hit resolution | `HitboxComponent` / `HurtboxComponent` | overlaps + queued sweeps | health, `hit_landed` |
| 30 | Economy | `ShardField` (§4.3; built, sets 30 in `_ready`), Sepulchre (as built in PR 4 it keeps the default priority 0: Grubs call `deposit()` and a Charge's hit reaches `take_charge_hit()` through the hurtbox, both from stage 0; its own tick only runs the deposit pulse) | shard pool, deposits | shard positions, segment count |
| 40 | Director | `SpawnDirector` (§4.5), `WakingController` (§4.8) | budgets, phase | spawns, stand-downs, phase changes |
| 50 | Warp | `WarpComposer` (§5.1) | tier inputs (WARP-001 §1.3) | active effect set |
| 60 | HUD model | `HudModel` (§5.2) | everything above | the honest readout |

Rule: a stage may read anything earlier in the same tick and anything from the previous tick; it may not write state owned by another stage. Signals emitted inside a stage are handled by listeners in a later stage or deferred.

### 4.2 Where state lives

| State | Class (LOOP-001 §2) | Owner | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|---|
| Seam index, seed, Sigils, Relics, Pact, Charges, Composure, in-run currency | Run | `RunSession` autoload (**planned**, §2.8) holding a `RunState` resource | New Vigil → death / run clear |
| Hourglass Clock, sky count, Hunger, corruption map, telemetry counters | Iteration | `RunSession` → `IterationState` resource | Iteration start → Hard Unmaking |
| Lantern Tree, banked currency, Codex, endings, bests | Anchored | `SaveService` profile slot, loaded into a `ProfileState` resource at boot | forever |
| Per-sector actors, shards, Sepulchre, warp effects | none (rebuilt from seed) | `SectorHost` children | one sector |

Sector scenes never own run state; they receive a `RunState` reference in `on_route_entered(params)` and write back through its methods. That is why a crash costs at most one Seam (LOOP-001 §2).

### 4.3 Shard field and the magnet loop
Shards are the one place a naive implementation scans N×M per frame (the Vigil's magnet plus every Grub looking for glass). **Built in M1 slice 3 PR 3** (`features/charges/shard_field.gd` + `.tscn`, `ShardFieldStats`, `data/charges/shard_field.tres`, keys in §3.4). Numbers are cited from SYS-001 §1.4, not copied:

| Piece | As built |
|---|---|
| Representation | **no node and no physics body per shard** (the earlier design said pooled `Node2D`; dropped: nothing per shard needs a node). Position, velocity, flags (alive, claimed by a Grub) and the last magnet tick sit in packed struct-of-arrays inside `ShardField`, indexed by a fixed pool with a free-slot stack and a dense live list. All positions are torus positions (the sector injects `Torus`; distances use `Torus.delta`) |
| Spatial index | uniform grid of **64 px cells** ✱ (one module, ART-002 §1) over the torus; per-cell intrusive doubly linked lists, updated when a shard changes cell. A torus size that is not a multiple of 64 gets a partial last row/column; a query span that crosses the wrap seam is split |
| Vigil magnet | circle query of SYS-001 §1.4 Shard magnet radius around the collector (the Vigil). Inside the radius the velocity steers toward the Vigil at the SYS-001 §1.4 pull speed and steer rate, **with no drag**; outside, SYS-001 §1.4 shard drag applies (PM-accepted; the Cabinet's `glass_field` was changed to match). Pickup when the distance ≤ Vigil hitbox radius + shard radius + pickup margin (SYS-001 §1.4 rows). With the magazine full the magnet is off and overflow glass stays (SYS-001 §1.4). **H4** (WARP-001 §3.2) = the `magnet_inverted` data flag, no second code path (no H4 caller yet) |
| Collection | one shard = one Charge into `ChargeMagazine.try_add` (cap from SYS-001 §1.4), plus score per shard (SYS-001 §7.1) |
| Grub targeting | hooks `grub_query_due`, `nearest_free`, `claim`, `release_claim`; Grubs may query once per 6 ticks ✱, not every tick. **Used since M1 slice 3 PR 4:** each `OublietteGrub` seeks the nearest free shard field-wide when `grub_query_due` (offset by its spawn index), claims it, and steals it with `take(i)` (the shard leaves the field; `stolen_total` counts steals); `is_claimed(i)` lets a seeking Grub notice that its claimed shard is gone (the Vigil collected it, or the slot was reused). A dying carrier spawns its shard back into the field; a claim is released on death |
| Worst case | pool **144** shards ✱ = 24 geodes (SYS-001 §9 cap) × 6, the detonation maximum (SYS-001 §1.3 detonation 4–6, Q-T29; calm kill 3–5 fits inside). Shards never despawn (SYS-001 §1.4), so the pool is sized for a field of detonations (Q-T59 resolved). **Pool exhausted:** `spawn` returns −1 and increments `spawns_refused`; a live shard is never recycled, nothing crashes |
| Tick | `process_physics_priority` 30, the Economy stage (§4.1) |
| Budget | ≤ 0.3 ms per tick at the worst case ✱ (§8.1); measured by `tests/perf/bench_shard_field` (§8.5) |
| Render | one `MultiMeshInstance2D` for all shards (one draw call) |

**Geodes feeding the field (built, `SectorGeode`, §2.6).** Accepted lance hits only. Calm mining: every SYS-001 §1.3 face-hits shatters a face and frees 1 shard; the last face frees the rest of the calm total (+1 mined bonus), at least 1. Over-fire: SYS-001 §1.3 over-fire hits inside its window detonate the geode (a warning cue once at the warn count); the detonated total (4–6) minus what already came out, at least 1, is drawn from the injected RunSeed `loot` stream (§4.9) and released so each shard comes to rest within the SYS-001 §1.3 scatter radius (release speed capped). Release kinematics and shape are the SYS-001 §1.3 ✱ rows. `GeodeSandbox` lays out the M1 geodes from the `map` stream (seam 0) and shapes from `vfx`, registers each with the `LedgerCompass` (kind `geode`), and each tick moves every geode to the image of its torus home nearest the Vigil (§4.1 priority 1). Cues: `geode_hit`, `geode_crack`, `geode_break`, `overfire_warn`, `overfire_burst`, `magnet_latch`, `shard_chime`, `charge_armed`, `magazine_full` (AUDIO-001 §12.13).

### 4.4 Freezing entities, never physics
The physics server is never paused for gameplay (§1.2 `physics_ticks_frozen = never`); `get_tree().paused` is used only by the pause screen.

| Freeze source | Mechanism | What keeps running |
|---|---|---|
| Anchor field (D-11, SYS-001 §1.1 `anchor_freeze`) | `FreezeComponent.freeze(duration, source_id)` on each unit inside the field (max units per cast from SYS-001 §1.1): disables the unit's brain and motor, zeroes velocity, mutes its chant emitter | its collision shapes (a frozen unit is an obstacle), its hurtbox (it can be killed), budget counting (SYS-001 §1.1 note) |
| Elites / Heralds | same, at half duration (SYS-001 §1.1) | — |
| Sepulchre, The Penitent | excluded by data flag `freezable = false` | everything |
| Pause screen | `SceneTree.paused` | Overlays (`PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS`) only |
| Codex / cinematics | `RunSession.clock_paused` (Hourglass Clock stops, WARP-001 §6.1 D-01); gameplay is paused by the same tree pause | — |

The freeze timer is owned by the field that cast it, so a unit leaving and re-entering a field cannot double-freeze or extend itself.

### 4.5 SpawnDirector and budgets
One `SpawnDirector` per `SectorHost`. It owns the SYS-001 §4 threat budgets and the SYS-001 §9 on-screen caps (Choir units, geodes, contacts, shots, colliders). Spawns over a cap are queued at off-screen spawn markers (ZONES-001 §1.7 `SpawnMarkerLit/Unlit`). Over the contact cap, the furthest units *stand down* (hold position, keep chanting) rather than despawning, because a vanishing enemy reads as a bug. The Coma mercy (SYS-001 §8) is a SpawnDirector rule keyed on `seam_deaths`.

### 4.6 Pooling and the shot layer
Shots, shards, sparks and chalk flecks are pooled at the SYS-001 §9 ceilings (180 shots both sides). Each shot sweeps a `ShapeCast2D`-equivalent query from its previous to its current position every tick (`PhysicsDirectSpaceState2D.cast_motion` with a pooled circle shape), so nothing tunnels (§2.6). The `ShotSweeper` batches all shots in one stage. Escalation path if it misses budget: batch fewer queries per shot type → grid broad-phase shared with §4.3 → quadtree (GDD §13 owner decision 2026-11-17) → GDExtension only with a profile and a §11 decision.

### 4.7 Choir AI
A unit is a `CompositeRig` (§2.4) + motor + `Brain` component. A `Brain` runs a small state machine whose states are `Resource`s (`state_patrol.tres`, `state_procession.tres`…), so a variant is a data edit. Unit stats and AI budgets come from SYS-001 §3; faction laws (SYS-001 §3.1: shrine ceasefire radius, Acolyte turret lag, Litany Ring) are **asserted** by tests, never left to tuning. Brains tick at 60 Hz for movement and at 10 Hz ✱ for decisions.

**As built (M1 slice 3 PR 4): Grubs only, no `Brain` or `CompositeRig` yet.** `OublietteGrub` is a plain `Node2D` with an enum state machine in code (seek, carry, flee, idle orbit), runtime-vector `GrubView`, numbers from `GrubStats` (SYS-001 §3). It decides every physics tick (no 10 Hz decision tick) but queries the shard field only when `grub_query_due` (§4.3). Rules: a carrying Grub never flees (Q-T112); a carrier joins the procession route at the nearest waypoint and deposits at the Sepulchre; a dying carrier drops its shard back into the field and keens (`grub_keen` only then; `grub_die` always). No RNG, so identical inputs give identical paths. `GrubProcession` spawns and respawns them (SYS-001 §3 respawn) and registers each with the Ledger Compass. Moving these rules into `Brain` state resources is owed when the second Choir unit arrives.

### 4.8 WakingController
One per sector with a Sepulchre. Owns the Waking lifecycle phases 0–6 (GDD §8.4, SYS-001 §2), the 20 segment modules and the Unravel hit count. Emits `phase_changed(phase)`, `segment_deposited(index)`, `segment_knocked_off(index)`, `entity_awakened`, `entity_unmade` (EVT-001 rows to add with the code). AUDIO-001's state lines, the HUD and the WarpComposer listen; the controller never calls them. The 1.2 s pre-Awakening silence (GDD §12) is a `phase_changed` consumer in audio, budgeted twice per run by the audio side. **Not built.** M1 slice 3 PR 4's `Sepulchre` (phases 0–1) counts segments itself, plays its cues through an injected callable and emits **no** signals; the signals above are added with the `WakingController` and their EVT-001 rows.

### 4.9 RunSeed, SeedCode and ReplayVerifier

| Piece | Design |
|---|---|
| `RunSeed` | a 55-bit integer. Every random draw goes through a **named stream** (`map`, `kits`, `slots`, `warp`, `loot`, `ai`, `vfx`): `stream_seed = hash64(run_seed, stream_name, seam_index)`, one `RandomNumberGenerator` per stream. Adding a roll to one stream never shifts another, so content patches break fewer seeds |
| Location (Q-T28) | `core/rng/`: `RunSeed` (`run_seed.gd`: masking, `stream()`, static `hash64`; there is no separate hash class), `SeedCode` (`seed_code.gd`: encode/decode, checksum). Seeds feed many features (map, warp, loot, AI, generators), so the code is feature-agnostic; `features/run` keeps `RunSession`/`RunState`. Tests in `core/rng/tests/` |
| `hash64` (Q-T26) ✱ | `RunSeed.hash64(run_seed: int, stream_name: String, seam_index: int) -> int` (static, owner ruling 2026-11-18, "M1 chat's version"). `h` = **FNV-1a 64** (offset `0xcbf29ce484222325`, prime `0x100000001b3`) over the UTF-8 bytes of `stream_name`. `x = run_seed XOR h XOR (seam_index * 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15)`. Then the **SplitMix64** finalizer: `z = (x ^ (x >> 30)) * 0xBF58476D1CE4E5B9; z = (z ^ (z >> 27)) * 0x94D049BB133111EB; result = z ^ (z >> 31)`. All arithmetic wraps at signed 64 bits; every `>>` is logical (GDScript has no logical shift, so the code masks the vacated high bits). The result is the signed 64-bit int. Constants are built from 32-bit halves because GDScript hex literals above int64 max are not portable. Pure integer maths, so identical on every platform |
| Seeds and `seam_index` (Q-T26) ✱ | `run_seed` is always masked to 55 bits (`RunSeed.SEED_MASK`; `RunSeed._init` masks); a fresh run takes 55 random bits from `Crypto.generate_random_bytes`. `stream_seed = hash64(run_seed, stream_name, seam_index)` with `stream_name` a String from the list above and `seam_index` an **int**: `0` for run-wide draws and when there is no seam (the run's map, world tier, Hunger preset, registry state), `1`–`5` for Seam 1–5 (LOOP-001 §2). The `map` stream uses `0` for the run's map and `1`–`5` for each Seam's layout. `stream_seed` is the full 64-bit result, assigned to `RandomNumberGenerator.seed` by `RunSeed.stream(name, seam_index)` |
| Determinism scope | world tier, Hunger preset and the WARP-001 §3.2 registry state derive from the seed alone (SYS-001 §10 🔒). Cosmetic `vfx` draws are seeded but excluded from verification |
| `SeedCode` | 12 characters of Crockford base32 (no I, L, O, U): 55 seed bits + 5 checksum bits = 60 bits. Displayed as `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`. A mistyped code fails the checksum and the UI says so in plain words (L10N-001 `MSG_` key) |
| `SeedCode` layout (Q-T26) ✱ | `SeedCode.encode(run_seed, grouped := false)`: value = `(run_seed << 5) \| checksum(run_seed)`, 60 bits, written as 12 symbols **most-significant first** (symbol 12 = bits 4–0 = the checksum). Alphabet in value order: `0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ`. `grouped = true` gives the display form `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`. `SeedCode.decode(code) -> int` is case-insensitive, strips hyphens and spaces, reads `I`/`L` as `1` and `O` as `0`, and returns `SeedCode.INVALID` (`-1`, never a legal seed) for `U`, any other symbol, a length other than 12, or a checksum mismatch. `is_valid(code)` wraps it. The checksum is the last symbol. Callers show a `tr("MSG_...")` line, never a raw error |
| Checksum (Q-T27) ✱ | `SeedCode.checksum(run_seed)` = **CRC-5**, polynomial x^5 + x^2 + 1 (`0x05`), init 0, no reflection, no final XOR, over the 55 seed bits fed MSB first (owner ruling 2026-11-18; replaces the XOR-of-5-bit-groups checksum, which ignored symbol order and missed adjacent swaps such as `C1SK` vs `1CSK`). Measured, not guaranteed: in the unit test 578/578 sampled adjacent swaps were rejected, and an independent Node check found 0 undetected in 212,958 random swaps |
| Test vectors ✱ | Pinned values from an independent BigInt Node script, matched by the GDScript. `hash64(123456789, "map", 0)` = `-7834688126535706054`; `hash64(123456789, "kits", 3)` = `-1513310087194196715`; `hash64(MAX_SEED = 2^55 - 1, "warp", -2)` = `-2734986135168668244`. SeedCode (CRC-5): seed `0` -> `0000-0000-0000`; `1` -> `0000-0000-0015`; `12345` -> `0000-0000-C1SK`; `2^55 - 1` -> `ZZZZ-ZZZZ-ZZZ4`. `core/rng/tests/test_run_seed.gd` asserts these |
| Cursed seeds | community-shared SeedCodes (GDD §6.3). A SeedCode carries no content version; the game states which content version a seed was minted on if it differs |
| Daily (post-1.0, ruling R-16) | `run_seed` derived from the UTC date `"YYYY-MM-DD"` and an int `content_version`, masked to 55 bits; the derivation is not yet specified (the superseded SHA-256 form is gone; the 3-argument `hash64` does not take a date, so decide when the daily is built); fixed world tier and Hunger preset; H14 never fires (WARP-001 §6.1 D-06) |
| Replay log | seed + content hash + per-tick quantised input: move vector (2 × int8), aim angle (uint16 of a turn), button bitfield (uint16). ≈ 6 bytes per tick ✱ → ≈ 21 KB per minute before compression ✱ |
| `ReplayVerifier` | re-simulates a log headless and compares a state hash (positions rounded to 1/8 px, health, segment count, clock) every 60 ticks. Used by tests and, later, to sanity-check daily scores |
| Honest limit | Godot 2D physics is not guaranteed bit-identical across CPUs and builds. Replays are verified **same build, same platform**. Leaderboards treat verification as a cheat filter, not proof |

---

## 5. UI and HUD

Menus, overlays, Theme, focus and input rules: UI-001 (built: attract, settings, pause, confirm). This section owns the in-run HUD and the warp renderer. Status: **designed, not built**, except §5.3's setting and pause gate and §5.4's Ledger Compass (with the compass slice of `HudModel`; `HudLiar` and `HudView` not built).

### 5.1 WarpComposer
One per sector. Single owner of which canvas-item shaders are active.

| Piece | Design |
|---|---|
| Inputs | the tier computation of WARP-001 §1.3 (iteration, sector consumed, Composure, Entropy) via `RunSession` |
| `WarpEffect` resource | `id` (W-code from WARP-001 §2), `tier`, `layer` (`world`, `units`, `hud`, `post`), `shader`, `params`, `t0_fallback` (ART-001 §7), `photosensitive_variant`, `reduced_motion_variant`, `cost_ms` (measured, §8.5), `clip_readable` (GDD §8.7 criterion 1, a review checkbox) |
| Layers | world and unit layers use per-`CanvasItem` materials; one `BackBufferCopy` + full-screen post pass at most ✱; the HUD layer has its own material so HUD warp never touches the world |
| Budget | sum of active `cost_ms` ≤ SYS-001 §9's 1.2 ms on min spec; over budget, the composer drops effects in WARP-001 §5.3's refusal ladder order and logs it (dev only) |
| Novelty cap | ≤ 3 novel effects per sector (GDD §16 R-02), enforced here |
| The Passing (W2-11 / W3-11, WARP-001 §2.7) | a world-layer warp event, scheduled here: at most SYS-001 §2.3 `Passing max per sector` (echo and live Passing together), charged SYS-001 §2.3 `Passing warp budget cost (first per sector)` against the novelty cap; refused in Seam 5, during a Rite and while a HUD lie is live (asks `HudLiar`). Lethality is a "lit" check on the Vigil, not a hitbox change. Lint flag `first_lethal_requires_echo` ✱ (§3.4) gates the first lethal W3-11 of a Chronicle on a witnessed W2-11, which needs a per-Chronicle "echo witnessed" bit in Anchored state (LOOP-001 §2). Cost fields owed (§11.1 Q-T72). Designed, not built |
| Determinism | effect start times come from the `warp` stream (§4.9) |
| Accessibility | photosensitivity swaps every flicker/strobe parameter for its static variant and **never** removes information (GDD §10); reduced motion swaps motion variants; both are read from `SettingsService` live. The Cabinet's S4 muzzle star is gated by `muzzle_flash` and `photosensitivity`, its recoil by `reduced_motion` (§2.10) |

### 5.2 DiegeticHud
Built as **model → filter → view**, so honesty is the default path and a lie is an explicit filter:

| Stage | Class | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Model | `HudModel` | the truth from §4 state, every tick. No warp code may write to it |
| Filter | `HudLiar` | applies WARP-001 §4.2 deviation classes (Lie, Smear, Glyph, Move, Absence) with exactly one tell family per lie, the per-sector lie budget by tier (T5 = 0) and the §4.2 laws L1–L12 (amended 2026-11-18, cosmic horror design rev 3): L6 refuses a lie on any resource spendable within SYS-001 §9 `Lie spend window (L6)`; L9 lets a lie *start* only in a lull (SYS-001 §9 `Lie lull radius`, `Lie lull intercept window`) and never during a Passing or its telegraph; L11 emits one onset cue per tell family; L12 keeps a short record of live lies so the death screen can name them (SYS-001 §9 `Lie death-attribution window`; `tr()` keys `MSG_DEATH_LIE_*`, NARR-001). Elements on WARP-001 §4.7's never-lie list, and the hull reads of L10 (Charge lamps and ring, cockpit-flame shape, Flame Bearing), are typed `HonestOnly`; the filter refuses them by assertion, and a lint test walks the element table |
| View | `HudView` | 64×64-module art (GDD §10), anchored inside the 200 % safe area (SYS-001 §9) |

Eaten elements (WARP-001 §4.3) are an `Absence` with the chalk frame still drawn.

### 5.3 SteadyLedger
`SettingsService` key `steady_ledger`, default **on** (WARP-001 §4.4) — **built**. With it on, `HudLiar` is bypassed for every category WARP-001 §4.4 lists; the Migrating HUD is disabled; the Hourglass vignette becomes a numeric run clock; glyph corruption is off. **Amended 2026-11-18 (cosmic horror design rev 3, CHD-05):** with it on, the Warp Tier and the Hourglass are **always** shown as honest numbers in the `top-left` anchor (WARP-001 §4.1, §4.4). With it off, the default HUD shows no Warp Tier number and no constant Hourglass outside Act 4 (the Hourglass appears at Seam boundaries, on death, in the Ruins and from SYS-001 §6 `Hourglass in-run show threshold`; WARP-001 §4.1). L10 hull reads and L12 death lines apply with it on or off; the Passing is a world event and stays. The **pause gate** is built: the `pause` action opens the real pause screen whenever the overlay stack is empty (`scenes/sector/sector_stub.gd`); future warp effects that eat the pause menu (W4-07) must check `steady_ledger` and stand down, enforced by a test when they are written.

### 5.4 Ledger Compass redraw
The compass draws the sector list, corruption state and up to 8 markers ✱. Static layers (sector cells, corruption bruise) redraw into a cached texture only when the macro map changes or at most 10 Hz ✱; markers are drawn per frame by one small `_draw` Control. Lies and H16 are applied in `HudModel`/`HudLiar`, never in the drawing code. Budget ≤ 0.2 ms per frame ✱ (§8.1).

**Built (M1 slice 3 PR 1, stub):** `LedgerCompass` (`features/hud/ledger_compass.gd`) with `LedgerCompassStats` (`data/hud/ledger_compass.tres`, `ds_hud.compass.max_markers` 8 ✱, `.redraw_hz` 10 ✱). Always honest: an `HONEST` constant and no lie logic (T0, WARP-001 W0-02; Q-T16 untouched). Placed top-centre, 4 px ✱ from the top of the sector HUD (size: see "beyond the stub" below). Markers: the Vigil first via the `vigil` group, then anything that called `LedgerCompass.register(node, kind)` (group `compass_marker`, kind in node meta), capped at `max_markers`. (The stub's "markers sampled at most `redraw_hz`" is superseded below.)

**M1 slice 3 PR 4 additions.** `register(node, kind, rank)` takes a compass rank (meta `compass_rank`, default 100 ✱); markers after the Vigil are listed by rank, lowest first, with a **stable** sort (`by_rank`), so under the `max_markers` cap the Sepulchre (rank 0 ✱) and its Grubs (rank 10 ✱) are never crowded out by geodes (default rank). An optional injected `torus` wraps each position into the sector before mapping (actors sit on the image nearest the Vigil, possibly outside the torus rect); without one, off-map targets are clamped as before. Kinds `sepulchre` and `grub` draw in `Pal.CHOIR_4` (Choir hue, ART-001 §4). Still always honest.

**Built beyond the stub (M1, branch `claude/m1-ledger-compass`, 2026-11-19).**
- **Size (Q-T120):** one **64 × 64** module (ART-001 §12.5 wins over the stub's 64×36), still top-centre (WARP-001 §4.1), `ds_hud.compass.size_px`. Runtime vector, `Pal` colours only: brass bezel (`GAS-2` with `GAS-1` keylines, `bezel_px` 4 ✱), `VOID-1` face, chalk tick marks (`BONE-5`) at the quarter points of each side. Layout (`LedgerCompassLayout`, 4 px sub-grid): face 4,4 56×56; map (the sector cell) 8,8 48×20; sector-list cell 8,36 16×8; entity frame 40,36 16×16.
- **Static layer** (`LedgerCompassStatic`): frame, ticks, the sector cell (the whole 1920×720 torus map, `VOID-0`, `BONE-2` keyline) with a grid of one cell per `grid_world_px` (640×360 ✱, one screen per cell: 3 × 2 on the M1 torus), the sector list and the entity frame. It redraws only when invalidated (`world_rect`, `torus` or size changed, or `invalidate_static()`), coalesced to at most `redraw_hz` (10 ✱). **The cache is the canvas item's retained draw-command list**, not a texture: Godot replays a `CanvasItem`'s recorded commands until `queue_redraw()`, so between invalidations the layer costs no script time (§11.2). A texture cache (SubViewport) is not needed at this size; revisit if the corruption bruise needs shader work.
- **Marker layer** (`LedgerCompassMarkers`): one small `_draw` per frame. Markers are now sampled **every frame** (the stub sampled at ≤ 10 Hz); `redraw_hz` throttles the static layer only. Dot radius `marker_radius_px` 1.5 ✱.
- **Entity marker frame:** drawn (chalk `BONE-5`) and **empty**. There is no entity in M1; `HudModel.entity_marker_visible` is false. H16 will keep it empty later (WARP-001 §3.2, §4.7 exception); PACT-19 may restore it.
- **Sector list:** a **one-cell stub** (the current sector, `PHOS-1` fill, `BONE-3` keyline). No doc specifies a sector entry's fields, so none are invented; `HudModel.sector_list_cells` = 1. Fields come with ZONES-001's macro map.
- **Honesty seam:** `HudModel` (`features/hud/hud_model.gd`, the compass slice of §5.2's model only) samples the world (true positions, torus-wrapped; the Vigil's is `own_position`). The drawing reads `HudModel.presented()` only and never applies a lie. With `SettingsService` `steady_ledger` on (default) `presented()` is the honest model; with it off the state goes through `HudModel.lie_hook()`, the seam where `HudLiar` plugs in. `HudLiar` is **not built**, so the hook returns the honest state: no lie path exists. The own-position readout never lies (WARP-001 §4.7).
- **Not built:** the corruption layer, `HudLiar`, H16, W1-09/W3-02/W3-07/W4-09, sector-list fields.
- **Budget:** measured by `tests/perf/bench_ledger_compass` (§8.3, §8.5): p95 0.14–0.20 ms per frame on a desktop debug build, inside the 0.2 ms ✱ budget. **p95 is the budget statistic** (PM under owner autonomy grant 2026-11-19). p99 and max spike over it in some runs (up to 0.365 ms p99 and 2.65 ms max); this is an M3 watch item, to be re-measured on a release build (PROD-001 §3 M3).
- **Cache (PM under owner autonomy grant 2026-11-19):** the static layer is cached as Godot's retained canvas draw-command list, not a texture; this counts as the "cached texture" above for now. Move it into a SubViewport texture when the corruption bruise needs one.

### 5.5 Rendering ladder (ART-001 §15)
Fidelity grows with the warp; the WarpComposer owns the switches.

| Stage | Enabled | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| T0 / Act 0 | flat colour through `Look.c()` (Neon Cabinet palette), CRT post | `features/cabinet` (built) |
| THE PRETENCE | `Look.t` 0 → 1: every cabinet colour morphs neon → Aether | `PretenceReveal` (built) |
| T1+ | **the Aether** (ART-001 §16): layered aether-fog shader fields with colour and currents, the crystal-sphere parallax, ambient aether-life particles; `PointLight2D` lantern (key light) + `LightOccluder2D` shadows; soft particles (`GPUParticles2D`, generated textures) | `features/aether` layers (**built: gallery pass 1**, dev-only route); `WarpComposer` applies `AetherLook` (planned). The reveal's first aether fog is `shaders/aether_fog.gdshader` (cabinet, built) |
| T2+ | emitter lights (Shroud, glass, veins), 2D HDR glow/bloom, dense particle systems, Shroud field shader | `features/aether` (life particles, glow in the gallery: built); `WarpComposer` (planned) |
| T3+ | volumetric fog and god-rays, distortion/lensing, normal-mapped generated bodies | `features/aether` (light shafts, leviathan, sphere refraction: built in the gallery); `WarpComposer` (planned) |

**Aether pass 2 note.** Any `AetherShaderRect` can render offscreen in its own HDR `SubViewport` (`own_world_3d`) at a fraction of resolution, shown by a `TextureRect` in the same draw slot; the quality ladder uses this for fog at low. Lantern light on fog is computed analytically in the shader (in-scatter), so it does not depend on the `PointLight2D`; the `PointLight2D` stays only as the lantern key light the lantern-key test asserts.

Forward+ (§1.1) supports 2D lights, shadows and HDR 2D. Every rung has a cost line in §8.1; on min spec the composer drops the costliest rung first, never a readability rule (ART-001 §15.1).

---

## 6. Asset consumption

> **D-13 / D-14 (2026-11-18): the game loads no image or audio files.** Every visual comes from a generator (ART-001 §14) and every sound from synthesis (AUDIO-001 §11). §6.1–§6.4 below describe the retired raster pipeline and remain only as reference. The current rule is §6.5.

Producer side: ASSET-003. This section is what the engine does with the output.

### 6.1 Inputs
(Retired raster inputs, now local-only and Godot-ignored:) `assets/sprites/SPRITE_MANIFEST.csv` (schema: ASSET-003), PNGs under `assets/sprites/generated/<kit>/`, atlases under `assets/sprites/atlas/`, `assets/palettes/darkstar_palette.json`. The manifest CSV is imported with importer **`keep`** (ruling R-19): Godot otherwise treats any CSV as a translation table. Fixed 2026-11-17 in `SPRITE_MANIFEST.csv.import`.

### 6.2 Consumer-side lints (planned as tests in `tests/assets/`)
Naming (ART-002 §2 grammar), palette (every row's colours ⊆ its kit's `kit_locks`, ≤ 10 colours, GASLIGHT only on the three ruling R-06 parts), existence (every `generated` row has a file), size (module multiples of 64 or the row's declared w×h). A lint failure fails the test suite; it never silently substitutes art.

### 6.3 ManifestImporter and AtlasTable (retired, D-13)
`core/assets/manifest_importer.gd` (planned), run headless (`-s`) as the `manifest_import` build step (§1.4). It strips the UTF-8 BOM the generator writes, merges `assets/sprites/SPRITE_MANIFEST_HAND.csv` (hand-authored rows the generator must never overwrite, ASSET-003), and fails on a duplicate id. Atlas sidecar JSON format: ASSET-003 §6.2. It reads the manifest and atlas JSON and writes `generated/sprite_table.gd`: a `const` dictionary `id → {atlas, region, pivot, sockets, frames}` (`AtlasTable`). Runtime code addresses sprites by manifest id only. A missing id in a dev build draws a VOID-5/BONE-5 checkerboard module and logs an error; in a release build the lint above has already failed the build.

### 6.5 Generators (current)
- **Visuals (build time):** pxl renders from `art/src/*.px.js` in `assets/art/generated/` (PNG, sheets, `SpriteFrames` `.tres`), on the palette (D-13 amended, ART-001 §14.8). They load as ordinary textures and swap between tiers.
- **Visuals:** generator classes draw through `CanvasItem` or shaders, with colours from `Pal` (`core/ui/pal.gd`) and text from `PixelText` on the Act 0 screen, `VectorText` everywhere else (§2.9a). A generator is deterministic from its parameters and seed; the WarpComposer (§5.1) feeds it `tier` and `horror`.
- **Sound:** `AudioService` plays the chirp render of each cue (`res://assets/audio/generated/sfx/<id>.wav`, built from `audio/src/*.cx.js`, D-14 amended) and builds an `SfxSynth` fallback lazily, only for a listed cue whose render is missing (AUDIO-001 §12.1; with all renders present, no synth cue is built); renders are mono except the four reverb cues (AUDIO-001 §12.2); long stems and beds render on a worker thread; reactive textures use `AudioStreamGenerator` (AUDIO-001 §11.5).
- **Enforcement:** `tests/test_generated_assets_only.gd` fails if any scene, script or resource under `features/`, `scenes/`, `core/`, `autoload/` or `data/` references a font, an image outside `assets/art/generated/`, or an audio file outside `assets/audio/generated/`; if any render has no matching `.px.js`/`.cx.js` source; if a pxl source does not include a project palette; or if a source uses pxl `image()` or chirp `sample()`.

### 6.4 CompositeRig consumption
`CompositeRig` (§2.4) reads part descriptors (ART-002 §5.1) and instantiates the part generators they name (§6.5); runtime mutation (ART-002 §5.3, the warp's limb swaps) swaps atlas regions on existing `Sprite2D` nodes, never instantiates nodes per frame.

## 7. Save system

### 7.1 SaveEnvelope
`core/save/save_envelope.gd`. A JSON object: `format`, `schema_version`, `slot`, `game_version`, `written_unix`, `checksum` (SHA-256 of the canonical payload), `payload`. Load statuses: `OK`, `MISSING`, `CORRUPT`, `CHECKSUM_MISMATCH`, `NEWER_VERSION`, `WRONG_SLOT`.

### 7.2 Slots and cadence

| Slot | Persistence class (LOOP-001 §2) | Allowed save reasons | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `profile` | Anchored | `ruins_entry`, `tree_purchase`, `iteration_end`, `cabinet_score` (Act 0 high score / game over), `cabinet_tape` (coin-slot hazard tape removed; written in attract, never mid-run), `cabinet_stage` (Act 0 furthest stage reached, written at each stage start: a boundary, Act 0 is not mid-run; P1b, Q-T133) | built: Act 0 writes `cabinet_hi_score`, `act0_complete`, `pretence_seen`, `cabinet_tape_peeled`, `act0_stage_reached` (int, −1 = nothing to continue; −1 at the reveal; §2.10). The shift total is run-scoped and never saved (Q-T132) |
| `iteration` | Iteration | `ruins_entry`, `iteration_end`, `new_iteration` | interface built |
| `run` | Run | `seam_boundary` only (never mid-run, SYS-001 §8) | interface built |

Profile flags for endings, Archivist variant and achievements are LOOP-001 §11.7 (invariant IDs). `act0_clean_hands` is evaluated **per Act 0 playthrough** (a replay from the Cabinet starts a fresh evaluation) and only ever sets the profile flag true.

`SaveService.save_slot(slot, payload, reason)` refuses any other reason with `save_failed(slot, &"not_allowed_now")`. Payloads hold invariant IDs and numbers only, never display text. No gameplay writes any slot yet.

### 7.3 Storage, recovery, migration
`FileSaveBackend` writes `user://saves/<slot>.json` via `.tmp` then rename, keeping the previous file as `.bak`. A corrupt or tampered main copy loads from `.bak` (`recovered = true`). A newer schema is refused and never overwritten. Migration: bump `SCHEMA_VERSION`, add one step in `SaveEnvelope.migrate`, never edit a shipped step. `MemorySaveBackend` is for tests. Steam Cloud is a future backend; not built. In test mode the default file root moves under `user://_test/` (§7.4).

Device settings live in `user://settings.cfg` (`SettingsService`), not in a save slot (ruling R-05, §11.1a).

### 7.4 Test isolation
Test runs must never write the player's real `user://` files. `core/save/test_mode.gd` (`class_name TestMode`, static only) ships but is inert in release.
- `TestMode.decide(debug_build, env, args)`: test mode is on only in a debug build AND (env `DARKSTAR_TEST_MODE=1` OR any command-line/user arg contains "gdunit", case-insensitive). An exported release build can never enter it.
- `TestMode.active()` caches the decision. `TestMode.force(on)` overrides it; turning it on wipes `user://_test` once per process, so each run starts from defaults.
- In test mode `SettingsService.settings_path` and the default `FileSaveBackend` root (`SaveService.backend`) redirect to `user://_test/settings.cfg` and `user://_test/saves`. `--no-save` is unchanged.
- Write guard: `TestMode.allow_write(path)` is called at the write chokepoints (`SettingsService._save`, `FileSaveBackend.write_text` and `remove`). In test mode a write to a real player path is refused (`ERR_UNAUTHORIZED`) and recorded in `TestMode.blocked_writes`. Outside test mode it always allows.
- Guard tests: `core/save/tests/test_user_data_isolation.gd` (paths under `user://_test`; the `decide` truth table incl. release = false; a deliberate real-path probe is refused and recorded; `persist = true` lands under `_test`) and `tests/test_zz_user_data_untouched.gd` (runs last; `blocked_writes` must be empty, so a test that hard-codes a real path fails loudly).
- Not verified: a plain windowed launch outside tests (code read only).

**Demo → full game (MARKET-001 §6, PROD-001 M4):** the demo build writes the same slots with the same `SaveEnvelope` schema and an extra payload field `content_scope = "demo"`. The full game reads demo saves through the normal migration path, clears `content_scope`, and keeps every anchored unlock earned in the demo. A demo build refuses a save whose `content_scope` is absent (a full-game save) instead of overwriting it, and says so in plain words. Both builds use the same Steam app data folder only if the demo is a Steam playable-demo branch of the same app (TECH-001 §1.4); otherwise the full game offers a one-time import from the demo folder.

Player-facing failure text: `PlayerMessages.save_message(reason)` → `MSG_SAVE_*` keys. Codes and paths never reach the screen; details go to `push_warning` only.

Troubleshooting: delete `%APPDATA%\Godot\app_userdata\DARKSTAR\saves\` to reset; a `.bak` next to a slot is the previous good copy.

## 8. Budgets

**Almost nothing in this section has been measured** (only the Aether gallery run and the shard-field bench in §8.5). Every number below is a target ✱ or a SYS-001 citation. Measurements go in §8.5 with a date, build and machine, or they do not exist.

### 8.1 FrameBudget
Target from GDD §15: median frame ≤ 8 ms at T2 and ≤ 14 ms at T5 on minimum spec (GDD §13: a 2016-class laptop iGPU for T0–T2; a GTX 1060-class GPU for T3–T5). The 8 ms T2 split ✱:

| Subsystem | Budget (ms) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Gameplay GDScript (motors, director, Waking, economy) | 2.0 | ✱ |
| Choir AI decisions | 0.8 | ✱ (§4.7, 10 Hz decisions) |
| Shot sweeps + hit resolution | 0.8 | ✱ (§4.6, at SYS-001 §9's 180 shots) |
| Shard field (§4.3) | 0.3 | ✱ |
| Warp effects | 1.2 | **SYS-001 §9** 🔒 |
| Other rendering (sprites, MultiMesh, particles) | 1.9 | ✱ |
| HUD + Ledger Compass (§5.2, §5.4) | 0.5 | ✱ |
| Slack | 0.5 | ✱ |
| **Total** | **8.0** | GDD §15 |

Not yet lines in this table, to be added when measured (Q-T24): **MSAA 2D 4×** on the root (§1.2) and the **display-resolution CRT** pass on the Act 0 screen (it now runs at window resolution, not 640×360). Neither has been measured; fallback if over budget: MSAA 2× ✱.

At T5 the 14 ms allowance is spent on warp and post passes; gameplay lines keep their T2 budgets.

**Aether backdrop (2026-11-18).** The whole `AetherBackdrop` is not yet a line in the table above. First numbers are in §8.5 (gallery, RTX 4070, 1080p; not min spec, not Deck). Required (CH-7): a quality ladder `ds_aether.quality` high / medium / low, where `low` is the Steam Deck / iGPU fallback (fewer fbm octaves, half-res fog bands, static god-rays, fewer particles) that **keeps the sphere curve, the entity's black and the play-band fog cap**; each layer toggleable with its own measured frame time. The backdrop must fit inside the Warp effects (1.2 ms) plus Other rendering (1.9 ms) lines, or the table is re-split by a §11.2 decision. The ladder is built (pass 2, §3.4) and a per-layer sweep exists at high (§8.5); the low fit is **not established** (the only low figure is an unreliable estimate; §11.1 Q-T62, Q-T90), and the medium rung is not measured.

No audio row exists above. The only audio budget is boot generation ≤ 1.5 s ✱ (AUDIO-001 §11.5). **Still owed:** a boot-time measurement of `AudioService._ready()` (the lazy fallback, AUDIO-001 §12.7 C1, removes the synth build when all renders exist, but the saving was not measured), audio CPU per frame, and loaded-cue RAM. Nothing audio-related has been measured; the method is in AUDIO-001 §12.8.

### 8.2 ContactBudget
The runtime counter for SYS-001 §9's caps (on-screen Choir units, entity modules, geodes, simultaneous hostile contacts, active shots, colliders per sector). Owned by `SpawnDirector` (§4.5). Over-cap behaviour: queue off-screen (spawns), stand down (contacts), refuse the Sigil shot multiplier past the shot cap (LOOP-001 §4.3 ceiling lint). Each cap gets a test that drives a bench scene to the cap and asserts the behaviour.

### 8.3 Measurement method
- Custom monitors: `Performance.add_custom_monitor("darkstar/<subsystem>_ms", …)` around each §8.1 row, visible in the editor debugger and dumped headless. **Not built** (none registered yet).
- Bench scenes: `tests/perf/bench_<subsystem>.tscn` drive each subsystem to its SYS-001 §9 cap for 60 s with a fixed seed and write p50/p95/p99 frame and subsystem times to `reports/perf/<date>_<build>.json`. Built so far: `tests/perf/bench_shard_field.tscn` (M1 slice 3 PR 3): full 144-shard pool, magnet always on (collected shards respawned outside the timing), collector circling the torus, 24 Grub queries per 6 ticks spread over the ticks; times `ShardField` per tick and the MultiMesh upload separately (the upload belongs to the "Other rendering" row). Run: `<godot> --headless --path . res://tests/perf/bench_shard_field.tscn`. `tests/perf/test_bench_shard_field.gd` keeps the harness runnable in the suite (short run, asserts no timing). `tests/perf/bench_ledger_compass.tscn` (2026-11-19): 16 moving targets on the M1 torus (Vigil, Sepulchre, 4 Grubs, 10 geodes), cap 8, static layer invalidated every frame (so it redraws at its 10 Hz ceiling), 3600 frames; per frame it times `LedgerCompass.tick` plus that frame's `_draw` passes (the layers' own timers). Run: `<godot> --headless --path . res://tests/perf/bench_ledger_compass.tscn`; `tests/perf/test_bench_ledger_compass.gd` keeps it runnable. The perf job's regression check is not built.
- Machines: a named minimum-spec machine per GDD §13 tier and a Steam Deck (GDD R-06, PROD-001 M3 gate). Results are only quoted with the machine named.
- A regression > 10 % ✱ on any p95 subsystem time against the last recorded run fails the perf job (CI, PROD-001).

### 8.4 GDScript hot spots and the escalation ladder
The two designed hot spots are the shard field (§4.3) and the Ledger Compass (§5.4); the third candidate is the shot layer (§4.6). The ladder for any miss, in order: fix the data structure (grids, packed arrays) → reduce frequency (decision ticks, cached layers) → batch rendering (MultiMesh) → move per-pixel work to a shader → GDExtension, only with a profile attached and a §11.2 decision.

## 9. Automated tests

Framework: **GdUnit4 v6.2.3** vendored in `addons/gdUnit4` (owner decision 2026-11-17). `addons/godot_ai` is vendored editor tooling and is not tested by the suite. Tests live in `<area>/tests/test_*.gd` next to the code (`core/**/tests`, `features/<f>/tests`, `scenes/tests`) plus cross-cutting suites in `tests/`. Helpers: `tests/support/` (e.g. `FakeRouteHost`, which lets UI tests trigger routing without replacing the runner scene).

```
tools\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe --headless --path . --import
tools\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe --headless --path . -s addons/gdUnit4/bin/GdUnitCmdTool.gd -a core -a features -a scenes -a tests --ignoreHeadlessMode -c
tools\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe --headless --path . --quit-after 600 -- --smoke
```
The smoke run prints `BOOT_SMOKE: reached route 'cabinet'` and exits 0. GdUnit writes HTML/XML reports to `reports/` (generated, not source).

**Test isolation (never touch player files).** Tests run in `TestMode` (§7.4): settings and saves redirect to `user://_test/`, and a write to a real player path is refused and recorded; `tests/test_zz_user_data_untouched.gd` fails the run if any was attempted. A new test that needs real file IO must use `user://_test/` or its own folder, never `user://settings.cfg` or `user://saves`. A run without a gdunit arg (for example another runner started from the editor) must set env `DARKSTAR_TEST_MODE=1` or call `TestMode.force(true)`. `persist = false` is no longer required for safety (still fine to set).

| Suite | Covers |
|---|---|
| `core/save/tests/test_user_data_isolation.gd` | test mode active, paths under `user://_test`, `decide` truth table, real-path probe refused and recorded, `persist = true` lands under `_test` |
| `tests/test_zz_user_data_untouched.gd` | runs last: `TestMode.blocked_writes` is empty |

Result on 2026-11-18 (attract boot / coin slot): 118 test cases, 0 failures, 0 errors; boot smoke (`-- --smoke --no-save`, attract → cabinet) exit 0 with no `ERROR`/`WARNING` lines. Not verified: real gamepad hardware, real mouse-drag feel, audio by ear.

Not covered (honest list): native-resolution rendering after Act 0 (decided, not implemented, D-A), the real window at any size (the Steam-survey sizes of §1.3 are covered as pure arithmetic in `test_screen_fit` / `test_bezel_art`, not by rendering them), gamepad glyphs (none exist), shaders, audio, Steam, performance. Slopes, stairs, doors and moving platforms are N/A (§2.6).

### 9.1 Automation runner (dev/QA)

Placed under §9 because it is a test tool (a scenario runs in the suite and from the CLI); it is not a runtime service, so it is not in §2.8.

**Purpose.** Drive the game from outside the player's hands: scripted input, UI actions, waits, asserts, screenshots. **No networking; D-09 stands.** The runner works in-process (a scenario file read by the game itself); there are no sockets, ports or servers.

**Boundary.**
- Code lives in `core/automation/` (`automation_gate.gd`, `scenario_runner.gd`, `scenario_schema.gd`, `input_driver.gd`, `ui_view.gd`, `capture.gd`, `error_capture.gd`, `caps_writer.gd`, `step_catalog.json`), tests in `core/automation/tests/`. No file declares `class_name`.
- No autoload. `ScenarioRunner` is a child of Boot, loaded **by path**, only if the launch args contain `--scenario=<id>` **and** the build has feature `editor`, `automation_dev` or `automation_qa`. Game code never references it, except Boot's guarded loader.
- New signal: `ScenarioRunner.scenario_finished(outcome)` (dev/QA only; EVT-001).

**Launch.** `godot --path . --fixed-fps 60 -- --scenario=<id> --no-save --out=<dir>`. The Node CLI builds this line: `node tools/automation/darkstar_auto.js validate` and `... run <id> [--windowed]` (see `tools/automation/README.md`).

**`--route=<id>` dev switch (M1 slice 3 PR 1).** `-- --route=<id>` makes Boot start on that routed scene instead of the table's initial route (e.g. `--route=sector` reaches the M1 sector without the Cabinet); combines with `--hull=1|2` and `--no-save`. `LaunchRoute` (`core/routing/launch_route.gd`) resolves it, with the **same gate as the runner** (feature `editor`, `automation_dev` or `automation_qa`); a release build, an unknown id or an empty value falls back to the initial route. `--smoke` ignores it (the smoke run still reaches `'cabinet'`). Routes stay ids from `data/routes/route_table.tres`, never paths.

**Scenarios.** `tests/scenarios/<id>.scenario.json`. The format is checked by `scenario_schema.gd`. **`core/automation/step_catalog.json` is the single source for the step list** (name, arguments, meaning); read it there, do not copy it here.

**Ids.** Ids only, never display text. UI controls: `<route>/ui/<Name>` (for example `attract/ui/NewVigil`). Read-only game state: the Cabinet ids listed under "Cabinet probe and dev ops" below (served by the cabinet probe). Routes without a probe keep their state ids in `ui_view.gd`'s `STATE_IDS` table: M1 slice 3 PR 1 adds `sector/vigil` (route `sector`; props `vigil_x`, `vigil_y`, `vigil_speed`, `hull_max_hp`, `camera_x`, `compass_markers`; later `camera_gap`, `facing_deg`, `aim_x`, `aim_y`), used by `tests/scenarios/m1_sector_feel.scenario.json`. M1 slice 3 PR 3 adds `sector/field` (route `sector`; props `geodes_alive`, `geodes_detonated`, `shards_free`, `charges`, `score`) and three suite-eligible scenarios: `m1_geode_mine` (aim right, feathered fire: calm mining frees glass, no detonation, no Charge), `m1_magnet_collect` (mine, fly in, the magnet collects: ≥ 1 Charge, score ≥ 200), `m1_overfire` (move up onto a 3-face geode's row, aim right with `mouse_move`, held fire: one detonation, ≥ 4 free shards). They rely on the sandbox seed (SYS-001 §1.3 "M1 sandbox geode layout"). `m1_mouse_aim` (`mouse_move`, then asserts `sector/vigil` `facing_deg`, `aim_x`, `aim_y`) covers the mouse path. M1 slice 3 PR 4 adds `sector/field` props `shards_live`, `charges_in_flight`, `charges_lost`, `charges_hit`, `charges_guttered`, `dry_fires`; a new id `sector/choir` (props `grubs_alive`, `grubs_carrying`, `segments`, `deposited_shards`, `sepulchre_complete`); and three suite-eligible scenarios: `m1_charge_fire` (dry fire at 0 Charges, mine and collect, fly down, fire: `charges_hit` ≥ 1 on the Sepulchre), `m1_charge_intercept` (collect, fire without flying down: `charges_lost` 1, no hit, no gutter), `m1_grub_procession` (4 Grubs, mine glass, a Grub steals it, about frame 348, and deposits it, about frame 686, as reported by the run; all 4 still alive). `m1_geode_mine` now waits on `shards_live` (shards lying in the world, claimed by a Grub or not) instead of `shards_free` (unclaimed only), because a Grub may claim the freed glass before the check. M1 Composure (Q-T118) adds `sector/vigil` props `composure` (0–10) and `composure_band` (`clear`, `frayed`, `unmoored`, `hollowed`); `m1_charge_intercept` now also asserts `composure` 10 / `clear` on entry and waits for `composure` 9.5 (band still `clear`) after the interception. A control's `<Name>` is its scene-unique `%Name` (UI-001 §7, §8).

**Mouse aim and injected input.** On the root window, `Viewport.get_mouse_position()` reads the OS cursor whenever the display server has a mouse (any windowed run), so motion injected with `Input.parse_input_event` (scenario `mouse_move`, replays) never reached aim windowed. Measured: injected logical (100, 60) while `get_mouse_position()` returned (838.5, -180) from the real cursor; headless falls back to Input's position, so it worked there. `VigilInput` now records each `InputEventMouseMotion` position in `_input` without consuming it (already logical 640×360 viewport space, root stretch undone) and converts it with the canvas transform inverse (`VigilInput.viewport_to_canvas`), the same mapping a real mouse gets. It falls back to `get_mouse_position()` before the first motion event; the right stick keeps priority. Real-player differences: aim holds the last in-window point while the cursor is outside the window; motion during pause is picked up on the next move. Scenarios should `mouse_move` right after the sector loads. Covered by `features/vigil/tests/test_vigil_input.gd` (7) and `tests/scenarios/m1_mouse_aim.scenario.json`. Follow-up (small, not done): `core/automation/step_catalog.json` calls `mouse_move` coordinates "window coordinates", but they are logical 640×360 (`input_driver` converts); fix the wording there.

**Determinism.** Waits count physics frames, each with a `within` limit. The CLI launches with `--fixed-fps 60`. The GdUnit path has no fixed-fps and asserts the outcome only (pass or fail), never frame counts or timing.

**Safety.**
- The runner refuses (exit 3) unless `SaveService.backend` is a `MemorySaveBackend`; it sets `SettingsService.persist = false` and resets settings to defaults before the first route.
- `--out` must be under `user://` or the project `reports/` folder, with no `..`; a bad path is refused (exit 3).
- No `eval`, no expression language, no console command execution: steps come only from the catalog.
- Held input is tracked in a ledger; every held key, button or mouse button is released when the scenario ends, on any outcome.

**Result.** `reports/automation/<id>-<stamp>/` holds `result.json`, screenshots and `console.log` (generated, not source). Exit codes: 0 pass; 1 fail or timeout; 2 invalid scenario; 3 refused (saves not in memory, or `--out` outside the allowed folders); a build without the feature tags prints one line and boots normally, ignoring `--scenario`; CLI only: 4 watchdog, 5 crashed.

**Capture scenarios.** `tests/scenarios/sizzle_s5_surge.scenario.json` (S5 Surgelight afterimage and wake, §2.10; suite-eligible, `screenshot` steps skipped headless).

**Suite adapter.** `tests/test_scenarios.gd` runs scenarios headless inside the GdUnit suite. It allows only scenarios with `args: []` and `timeout_frames` ≤ 1200 ✱.

**Aether scenarios (2026-11-18).** `tests/scenarios/aether_gallery_tiers` (steps through the tier exhibits) and `tests/scenarios/aether_gallery_entity` (the entity-presence exhibit) open the dev route `aether_gallery` with `goto`, so they only run in a dev build (§2.1). Both are suite-eligible; headless runs skip `screenshot` steps, so the windowed capture, the `AETHER_COST` line and any pixel checks happen only with `--windowed` (§11.1 Q-T64). Gallery log lines (windowed only): `AETHER_COST` (whole backdrop; now carries quality and px), `AETHER_LAYER_COST` (per layer), `AETHER_SWEEP_*` (sweep progress and summary). Entity-scenario and sweep runs end with "ERROR: 2 resources still in use at exit" (the entity run also "4 ObjectDB instances were leaked"); new in pass 2, cause not found (Q-T91, Q-T54). Related tests: `features/aether/tests/*`, `scenes/dev/aether_gallery/tests/*`, `tests/test_dev_routes_gate.gd`.

**Known limitations.**
- `--headless` has no renderer: `screenshot` steps are skipped. Use a windowed run (`--windowed`).
- Scenario args are read from the real command line only (Q-T47). Further consumers (Q-T47): the Aether gallery (`scenes/dev` only) reads `--aether-quality=high|medium|low`, `--aether-sweep` and (pass 3) `--aether-exhibit=N --aether-measure` itself (one exhibit per fresh process: 120 warm-up frames, average of 120, print `AETHER_COST`, quit); `ui_focus_next` cycles the quality in the gallery.
- The suite adapter limits above apply.
- `step_sim` steps the ArcadeWorld only; it does **not** advance the `CabinetGame` state machine or its timers.
- `ErrorCapture` is proved through the real `OS.add_logger` by `core/automation/tests/test_live_logger.gd`, which runs a child Godot process (`core/automation/tests/support/live_logger_probe.gd`) that raises a real `push_warning` and `push_error`. Known limit: the captured file and line are the engine call site, not the GDScript line. The support script is excluded from release and QA by the existing test globs.
- QA exe `--exe` runs are headless only, not windowed (see "Profiles and presets").
- Recorder replay is faithful only with the same stage seed, save state and `--fixed-fps 60`; determinism is not verified beyond a ±2-frame replay test. Raw keys not mapped to actions are not recorded.
- Headless runs that quit on the attract route end with Godot's "ObjectDB instances were leaked" and "resources still in use" lines. Cause not investigated; harmless (Q-T54).

**Profiles and presets.**
- Profile `dev` = build feature `editor` or `automation_dev`; profile `qa` = feature `automation_qa`. Catalog steps marked `"dev": true` are refused in QA (exit 3, before frame 0). A scenario may declare `profile` and can only ask for less: qa on a dev build is held to qa; dev on a qa build is refused. `result.json` holds `profile` (effective) and `build.profile`.
- QA saves: fixtures must be listed in `tests/fixtures/saves/approved.json`.
- New steps: `set_setting{key,value}` (dev only; SettingsService accessibility keys only); `reset{}` (both profiles: clears memory saves, resets settings, back to attract).
- `Windows Desktop` excludes `core/automation/*`, `features/*/automation/*`, `tests/*`, `core/*/tests/*`, `features/*/tests/*`, `scenes/tests/*`, `scenes/*/tests/*` (any scene folder's tests, e.g. the dev gallery's), `scenes/dev/*`, `data/routes/dev_*` (dev-only routes: the Aether gallery), `addons/gdUnit4/*`, `addons/godot_ai/*`, `reports/*`; `data/` stays shipped. `Windows QA` (feature `automation_qa`) includes `core/automation/step_catalog.json`, `tests/scenarios/*.json`, `tests/fixtures/saves/*.json` (json is not a resource, so it needs `include_filter`) and excludes test code, `tests/support/*`, gdUnit4, godot_ai, `reports/*`. The godot-ai export plugin strips its `_mcp_game_helper` autoload in headless export.
- Guard test `core/automation/tests/test_export_presets.gd`: every non-automation preset excludes the required globs and has no automation feature; the QA preset has `automation_qa`, the json includes, excludes gdUnit4/godot_ai, keeps scenarios and fixtures; each `core/automation` and `features/*/automation` folder is covered by the release excludes; no credential-like non-empty keys.
- Pack audit: `--export-pack "<preset>" build/audit/<x>.pck`, then `node tools/automation/pack_audit.js <file.pck> --profile release|qa` (exit 0 pass, 1 fail). Release must hold no automation, tests, gdUnit4, godot_ai, scenarios or fixtures; qa must hold the runner, `step_catalog.json`, scenarios, `approved.json`, and no gdUnit4, godot_ai or test code.
- Recorder: `-- --record=<id> --no-save [--out=<dir>]` (dev profile, memory saves only) writes `<out>/<id>.scenario.json` on exit: InputMap action press/release, mouse buttons and position, idle frames collapsed to `wait_frames`.
- Verified 2026-11-18: the exported release exe with `--smoke --no-save --scenario=a0_first_contact` prints one line "automation: not available in this build", reaches route `'cabinet'`, exit 0, no ERROR/WARNING. New scenarios `ui_settings_roundtrip` and `qa_reset` also pass in the suite adapter.
- QA exe from the CLI: `run <id> --exe=<exe>` and `run-all --exe=<exe>` spawn the exported exe directly, cwd = the exe folder, no `--path`. The exported game accepts `--out` only as `res://reports/...` (= `<exe dir>/reports`); an absolute path is refused (exit 3). QA builds refuse scenarios that use dev steps (exit 3); the CLI labels that "refused (QA profile)" and does not count it as a failure when the exe is a QA build, the result profile is qa and the scenario uses dev steps. Verified on a real `Windows QA` export, headless only: every QA-legal scenario passes in the exe (14 at the last run, 2026-11-19: all scenarios without dev steps, e.g. a0_first_contact in 513 frames, the Aether and M1 scenarios, sizzle_s5_surge, qa_reset, ui_settings_roundtrip); the 7 dev-step scenarios (`a0_acolyte_shot_death`, `a0_death_marks_clear`, `a0_death_reduced_motion`, `a0_entity_edge_death`, `a0_offview_killer`, `a0_shooter_gone`, `cabinet_probe_tour`) are refused with exit 3. The release exe still prints "automation: not available in this build" for `--scenario`.
- CI is a separate PR.

**Cabinet probe and dev ops (phase 2).**
- Probe: `features/cabinet/automation/cabinet_probe.gd`, no `class_name`, loaded by path from a const route-to-probe table in `scenario_runner.gd`. The folder is excluded from release by the `features/*/automation/*` glob; QA ships it and the pack audit requires it there. Duck-typed contract: `describe()`, `has(id)`, `read(id, prop)` returning `{ok, value, error}`, `snapshot()`, `dev_op(op, args)`, `event_names()`, `connect_events(sink)` / `disconnect_events()`. The runner reads Cabinet ids only through the probe; `ui_view.gd` keeps UI ids only.
- Ids and allow-listed props: `cabinet/game` (state, stage_index, demo); `cabinet/score` (score, lives); `cabinet/record` (grubs_destroyed, glass_mined, charges_thrown, glass_carried); `cabinet/vigil` (alive, shield, pos_x, pos_y, use_scripted, `killer_kind` "none"|"shot"|"entity" from the latest death mark via the same `death_big_killer_px` cutoff as Sizzle's S6, `killer_dx`, `killer_dy` (torus delta from the mark's death position to the killer), `killer_on_screen`, `shooter_id` (latest death mark's shooter, -1 none), `shooter_alive`, `shooter_on_screen`, `death_marks` count, `death_outline` and `death_arrow` = 0..1 chalk draw progress of the latest mark, 0 with none); `cabinet/world` (`last_spawned_acolyte_id` (-1 none, recorded by `spawn`), acolytes, grubs, geodes, shots, hostile_shots, charges, paused_sim, sim_scale, hitstop_left, time, death_marks, entity_awake, entity_dx, entity_dy); `cabinet/acolyte/<id>` (alive, hp, pos_x, pos_y, on_screen, dx, dy); `cabinet/grub/<id>` (alive, carrying, hp, pos_x, pos_y); `cabinet/geode/<index>` (alive, faces_left). A gone entity reads `exists:false`. Snapshot lists are capped at 200. `Acolyte.id` was added in `features/cabinet/sim/acolyte_squad.gd` (counter not reset on reset, like Grub ids).
- Events (from ArcadeWorld signals): `vigil_destroyed`, `score_awarded [points,x,y]`, `hitstop_started [beat]`, `slowmo_changed [scale]`, `sepulchre_cleared`, `entity_unmade`, `phase_changed [phase]`, `segments_changed [count]`, `charges_changed [count]`. The last 256 go in `result.json`.
- Steps: `snapshot{name}` (writes `snapshots/<name>.json`: UI list plus probe snapshot; max 50), `wait_event{event, within, count?}`, `perf_sample{}` (fps, process and physics ms, objects, nodes, orphans, static memory; max 200). Dev ops, all `dev` (refused in QA) and Cabinet route only: `set_stage`, `set_lives`, `teleport`, `spawn{kind: acolyte|geode|shard, dx, dy}` (relative to the Vigil), `shoot_vigil{dx,dy,acolyte_id?}` (a hostile shot from an offset, aimed at the Vigil; optional `acolyte_id`, int ≥ 0 or "last" = the Acolyte most recently made by `spawn acolyte`, is passed as the shot's `shooter_id`; omitted means no shooter; "last" fails plainly if none was spawned; the id of a dead or missing Acolyte is accepted, which stages "shooter gone"), `kill_all` (acolytes, grubs, hostile shots), `kill_acolyte{acolyte_id}` (int or "last"; kills exactly that Acolyte through `AcolyteSquad.kill()` and leaves shots alone, which `kill_all` cannot do; fails plainly if no live Acolyte has that id), `pause_sim`, `step_sim{frames}` (needs a paused sim), `place_entity{dx,dy}` (|v| ≤ 4000; fails plainly if the Sepulchre entity is not awake), `wake_entity{}` (via the public `SepulchreRitual.deposit()`: sets segments 19, shard_points 2, deposits; the entity is awake after about 4.2 s of sim time, so wait on `cabinet/world entity_awake`; fails on a Cold Sepulchre or if already waking; stage 0 is Cold, so `set_stage 1` first).
- Error capture: `error_capture.gd` (a Logger) fills `result.errors[]`, `error_count`, `warning_count`. Scenario fields `fail_on_errors` and `allow_errors` (substrings).
- Caps: `-- --automation-caps --no-save --out=<dir>` (guarded branch in `boot.gd`) writes `caps.json` through `caps_writer.gd`: profile, steps (with the dev flag), comparators, screenshot targets, probe descriptions, approved fixtures, build. CLI: `caps`, `run-all [--only=]`, `validate`.
- Scenarios: `a0_acolyte_shot_death` (real on-screen Acolyte at (0,-90) fires the staged shot as shooter; asserts `shooter_id` ≥ 0, alive, on screen), `a0_offview_killer` (Acolyte spawned off view at (0,-450) as shooter; asserts acolytes 1, shooter alive, shooter off screen), `a0_shooter_gone` (kills the shooter after the shot is in flight; asserts `killer_kind` shot, `shooter_id` ≥ 0, `shooter_alive` false; stills `gone_00/06/16/36/76` + `gone_window`), `a0_entity_edge_death` (set_stage 1, wake_entity, place_entity at dy -28, entity kill; stills `entity_00/06/16/36/76` + `entity_window`), `cabinet_probe_tour` (with the earlier three), `a0_death_marks_clear`, `a0_death_reduced_motion` (all pass headless via CLI and the suite adapter). They produce arcade stills `dead_00/06/16/36/76` and `offview_00/06/16/36/76`, plus window stills in windowed runs only. The death scenarios also assert `killer_kind` ("shot" or "entity") and `death_marks` ≥ 1. S6 chalk draws after a 0.5 s delay: partly visible from about frame 36, clearly by 76, nothing at +16, hence the `_76` stills.
- Findings: (1) the Vigil's respawn shield is already 0 when the state reaches play. (2) A real Acolyte rarely kills a stationary Vigil: it holds a 170 px ring around the Vigil (mostly outside the half-view), its fire timer runs only on screen, and its turret aims 0.25 s behind while orbiting; chasing it with real input failed too. So `a0_acolyte_shot_death` spawns a real on-screen Acolyte and stages the lethal shot with `shoot_vigil` from the same spot, passing that Acolyte as shooter (the shot-to-Acolyte link exists since Sizzle's `shooter_id`; Q-T74 resolved; earlier runs had no link). The chalk shows a ring on the shooter and a leader to it when alive; an alive off-view shooter gets the arrow plus a leader running off the view edge; with the shooter gone only the short arrow shows (no ring, no leader). Caveats: the spawned Acolyte wanders, so `shooter_on_screen` is asserted right after the Vigil dies, not at +76; an off-view shooter staged by `shoot_vigil` is staged, not natural (Acolytes never fire off-screen, owner ruling Q5). (3) A hostile shot from 90 px kills in about 22 frames, from 450 px in about 110. (4) An off-view killer is possible only by shot origin: the Vigil is always at the view centre and Acolytes never fire from off-screen. (5) Contact range is about 26 px plus the Vigil radius, so an entity body can never be partly off view when it kills; `a0_entity_edge_death` captures the kill with the leader line to the entity's live position instead (S6 case c as far as physically possible). A shot's death mark sits at the Vigil, so a shot's `killer_on_screen` is always true; an "off-view" shot only arrives from off-view, wrapping on the torus. (6) Death mark lifecycle (S6): marks never age out and are held through the whole DEAD state. They clear when the respawn happens (`lose_life()` true in `CabinetGame._on_timer`); on game over (last life) they stay. `world.start()` also clears them (via `fx.reset()`). After a respawn `killer_kind` is "none" and `death_marks` 0 (scenario `a0_death_marks_clear`). (7) Reduced Motion: the chalk outline and arrow complete at once after the 0.5 s delay (otherwise they draw on over about 0.15 s and 0.12 s), slow-mo is off and hitstop frames are halved. Scenario `a0_death_reduced_motion` (dev op `set_setting`): at +36 frames reduced motion shows the complete chalk while normal is mid-draw; by +76 both look the same apart from killer geometry.

**Adding automation support.**
- New focusable UI control: give it a scene-unique `%Name`; that is its id.
- New state id: add it to the table in `ui_view.gd` (UI) or to the feature's probe (game state).
- New step: add it to `step_catalog.json`, implement it in `scenario_runner.gd`, add a test in `core/automation/tests/`.

**Phases.**

| Phase | Content | Status |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Runner, gate, schema, catalog, input driver, UI view, capture, Node CLI, suite adapter, first scenarios | built (this branch; counts in the PR) |
| P2 | Cabinet probe, `Acolyte.id`, dev ops, `snapshot`/`wait_event`/`perf_sample`, error capture, caps, S6 capture scenarios | built (this branch). Not built: probes for entities beyond the ids above; (`shoot_vigil` forwards the shooter id and `kill_acolyte` exists; the shot-to-Acolyte link is built, Q-T74 resolved). S6 case c covered as far as physically possible (entity kill, leader line to the live position; a body partly off view cannot occur) |
| P3 | Release exclusion, QA preset and profile, preset guard test, recorder, pack audit, `set_setting`/`reset` steps | built (this branch), except that CI is a separate PR |

**Aether pass 4 guard test (2026-11-19).** `features/aether/tests/test_aether_pass4.gd` is a CPU proxy, not a render: it flood-fills the fog and chalk strand maths over 3 drift directions × 2 seeds × 2 times × 4 thresholds on a 110×110 grid and fails on any closed hole near the play centre, with a control proving the old ring construction is caught. It also bounds entity cracks inside a band just outside the mass (`A_ENT_CRACK_BAND` 0.24 × grow, checked for every radius and growth the backdrop produces), and checks the meridian numerals decode to exactly I to XII with only I, V and X stroke branches. Limits: no headless shader render (CPU mirror, double precision); the blend ring between play core and free fog, the opaque far band and colour terms are not covered. Enforces ART-001 §16.6c.

**Aether pass 4 ✱ engineering params** (shader / layer constants, not SYS-001 numbers): `A_FOG_STREAK` 3.2, `A_PLAY_CORE` 0.22, `A_PLAY_RING` 0.5, `A_ENT_CRACK_BAND` 0.24, `EntityPresence.crack_cells` 22 (replaces `tendril_count`), numeral layout `NUM_H` 5.0, `NUM_ROW` 10.4, `NUM_CLEAR` 9.0, T4 fracture `CELL_FRAC` 0.36, `FORE` 0.45, `OPEN_INSET` 1.6. New `AetherLook` flag `meridian_numerals` (default true).

**Aether pass 5 guard test (2026-11-19).** `features/aether/tests/test_aether_pass5.gd` has two parts, both CPU or analytic proxies, not renders. (1) Black only inside the mass: `entity_presence` writes black only where `s < 0`; world-side cracks, rims and fragments stay inside `0 < s < A_ENT_CRACK_BAND` × grow (ART-001 §16.6c). (2) Law 4 fog bound: an analytic bound mirroring the fog shader's lantern, drain and band-alpha terms. Fog beside the ship (play-core fog capped at `A_PLAY_FOG_LUMA`, lantern weight `BESIDE_LP`) must be at most `POOL_RATIO` × the dimmest lantern pool heart, and the cap itself under half the pool heart, for every look. Printed ratios: `drowned_observatory` 0.73, `green_rot` 0.66, `litany` 0.74, `pale_marches` 0.76, `the_black` 0.68. Not covered (Q-T122): grade, bloom, non-fog layers, the play RING (blend to free fog). Enforces ART-001 §16.3 and §16.6c.

**Aether pass 5 ✱ engineering params:** `A_FOG_DASH` 9 (play-core streak length), `A_PLAY_FOG_LUMA` 0.14 (cap on each band's own light over the play core, before lantern in-scatter), `BESIDE_LP` 0.5, `POOL_RATIO` 0.8 (test), `A_KEEP_INNER` 0.3, `A_KEEP_FRINGE` 0.18 (T4 remaining hairlines), `A_DIM_PANES` 0.16, `A_DIM_ALPHA` 0.5, `A_DIM_VALUE` 0.55 (T4 dim panes), `A_ENT_FRAGMENTS` 0.22, `A_ENT_FRAGMENT_FROM` 0.2, `A_LOSS_BITE_STEP` 2 (loss mask every second bite), `A_LOSS_REACH` 1.75 (plus AA width; skip proven conservative and tested), `A_STRIP_FULL` 0.8 (free fog skipped where strip weight is full).

**Relation to the `godot-ai` MCP.** `godot-ai` is an editor-time live tool (it talks to the open editor). This runner is separate: it runs the game by itself, from a scenario file, with no editor.

### 9.2 CI (GitHub Actions)

File: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (job `checks`). Status: added 2026-11-18; the first run is the PR that adds it, so it is not yet shown to pass.

**Setup.** Runs on every pull request and on pushes to `main`, on `ubuntu-latest`. Timeout 25 min ✱. Token is read-only (`permissions: contents: read`); no secrets. Concurrency cancels older runs of the same ref.

**Steps.**
1. Check out without LFS. Cache the LFS objects (key = hash of `git lfs ls-files -l`), then `git lfs pull`. Only generated audio is in LFS (about 25 MB, 45 files); on a cache hit nothing is downloaded, so no LFS bandwidth is used.
2. Download Godot 4.7.2-stable (Linux zip, official release), check it against a pinned SHA-512, cache the binary.
3. `--import`.
4. The GdUnit4 suite (`-a core -a features -a scenes -a tests`).
5. Boot smoke (`--smoke --no-save`): must print `BOOT_SMOKE: reached route 'cabinet'` and have no ERROR or WARNING lines.
6. Every `tests/scenarios/*.scenario.json`, headless, through `node tools/automation/darkstar_auto.js run <id> --godot=<exe> --keep` (§9.1).
7. On failure, upload `reports/` and `smoke.log` as an artifact (7 days).

Actions (checkout, cache, upload-artifact) are pinned to full commit SHAs (tags resolved 2026-11-18).

**Not in CI.** Exports and pack audits (they need the 1.2 GB export templates); a Windows runner (planned later, on demand or nightly: Windows minutes cost 2x and Windows is the ship target); screenshots (headless has none); the Web export.

**Cost.** Private-repo free-minutes allowance; Linux minutes count 1x. Measured on the first GitHub run (PR #28, cold caches): 66 seconds total, 401 test cases, 0 failures; the LFS and Godot caches missed and were saved, so later runs should be similar or faster. The minutes allowance is a non-issue at this scale. Measure again if the suite grows a lot.

**Run the same checks locally.** The commands are in CLAUDE.md §4 (import, suite, smoke), plus `node tools/automation/darkstar_auto.js run <id>` for scenarios.

**Update pins.**
- Godot: change the version and the SHA-512 together in `ci.yml` (take the hash from the official release's `SHA512-SUMS.txt`); bump the cache key with it. Keep it equal to the version in CLAUDE.md §4.
- Actions: resolve the new tag to its full commit SHA and replace the pinned SHA; keep the tag in a comment.

**Required check (recommendation).** After the first green run, the owner may mark the `checks` job required in branch protection. Owner's decision; not changed here.

## 10. Import conventions
**D-13:** no raster imports feed the game. The reference PNGs under `assets/sprites/` are local-only reference: kept on disk, **not in git** (`.gitignore`) and **ignored by Godot** (`assets/sprites/.gdignore`), so they are never imported, exported or pushed. Only `SPRITE_MANIFEST.csv` is tracked, as the inventory of parts to build (owner decision 2026-11-18). Exports must still exclude `tools/**` (set in the export preset when it is created). Historical, for the reference PNGs only: per ART-002 §7, canvas default filter Nearest (set in `project.godot`); pixel atlases import with `compress/mode = Lossless`, mipmaps off, `fix_alpha_border = true`. Every `.csv` under `assets/` imports as `keep` (ruling R-19), never as a translation; PNG import flags per ASSET-003. ART-002 §7 mentions a `godot/DarkStar/` path; the project root is the repo root (TASK-BOOT Q4), so that path is obsolete — §11 Q-T08. No import preset file is committed yet. `docs/`, `tools/`, `research/` and `Claude outputs/` carry `.gdignore`.
